689:## 12. Wave-24 loop repairs (DX7)
690:
691:Commission: Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; implementer: Codex in sb4-b24-dx7.
692:This amends §11, LLP 3000.036 §22.8 and the ordinary rule-helper exclusion.
693:SEC8 owns security questionnaire/dispositions, presence intent, invariant test
694:coverage and lifecycle advice; DX7 only reports their existing verdicts.
695:Implementation and tests accompany this amendment; review readiness is distinct
696:from author acceptance.
697:
698:### Durable staging time
699:
700:A staging store owns its cumulative offset and latest explicitly checkpointed
701:business time. The additive typed metadata record also retains a pending
702:maintenance pause. Production ignores this record and rejects clock controls;
703:ordinary dev retains its process-local clock/reset contract. No release-format
704:change or filesystem sidecar is needed. Store adapters use their existing
705:metadata transaction and fencing, including Postgres.
706:
707:Startup resumes at the maximum of wall plus saved offset, checkpointed time,
708:and the persisted time_audience_clock disclosure floor (LLP 3000.000.000.002
709:§4), plus the maximum retained commit stamp and schedule boundary even when a
710:checkpoint exists. A fresh store with no evidence starts at wall time. Unknown
711:metadata is preserved by ordinary old-binary adoptions; if an older binary or
712:manual restore removes it, recover time from these retained floors, with no
713:claim to recover a deleted pause. Explicit startup
714:offsets (serve --clock-offset and startServe({clockOffset})) are absolute
715:offsets from wall time, clamped to the resumed floor;
716:restarting never adds the same offset twice or rewinds saved boundaries.
717:The resulting effective offset is persisted at startup, preserving the saved
718:paused flag exactly. This is the maximum of five named evidence values: wall
719:plus offset, checkpointed now, time_audience_clock, recent commit maximum and
720:latest schedule boundary (plus an explicitly requested offset floor). Removing
721:all time evidence by restoring/recreating a store can return it to wall time;
722:this is the intentional independent-test reset path, never an in-place rewind.
723:Old binaries do not enforce the pause even when they preserve its record;
724:downgrade is not a way to run a paused proof under these guarantees. Malformed checkpoints
725:refuse startup rather than silently resetting. Downgrades to older binaries
726:ignore this additive record and retain their historical process-local behavior.
727:
728:Each accepted staging advance checkpoints offset, time and paused=true in the
729:same transaction that preserves eligible sessions and worker leases, before
730:publishing the process clock or doing maintenance. E_CLOCK_HELD and input validation precede this transaction. A definite
731:storage abort rolls back all those effects. Postgres executes the existing
732:fenced writer transaction containing offset/time/pause and session/lease edits.
733:An uncertain COMMIT may already have committed: E_COMMIT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN fences
734:the host from further admission/maintenance, then shuts it down. Restart reads
735:the durable checkpoint before serving; never replay a credential shift to
736:reconcile it. An uncertain pause-clear may already have cleared it, so only
737:definite failure guarantees retention of the durable pause. Fault injection
738:covers both write boundaries. A lost reply is still ambiguous and never automatically
739:replayed. drain:false, a failed drain and an interrupted drain retain the pause
740:across restart. A successful drain durably clears it only after due work has
741:finished. If that final write fails, report an incomplete drain and retain the
742:pause. Startup writes a checkpoint with zero delta and never preserves credentials.
743:Each advance computes its delta from the resumed effective clock and writes
744:credential timestamps and checkpoint in one commit: the checkpoint itself is
745:the durable completion evidence; there is no separate applied marker. A restart
746:does not shift already-preserved credentials again. Clock
747:status/advance reports say the offset belongs to this staging store. Coalescing,
748:eachBoundary, current-window equality and explicit allowHeld keep §11 semantics.
749:
750:### Rendered and scrollable text
751:
752:Mounted text inside overflow:auto/scroll ancestors counts even when outside
753:the scrollport: it can be reached by scrolling. The selector does not scroll
754:or change application layout. Overflow:hidden/clip remains a real clip, with
755:per-axis character geometry retaining only reachable characters. Starting at
756:each text rectangle, compose legal scroll shifts [current-max,current-min]
757:and each ancestor clip from inner to outer, independently for x and y; this
758:excludes unreachable negative-origin text and admits an inner scrollport
759:inside an outer hidden clip without scrolling the page. Zero-sized scrollports
760:remain clips. visibility:visible descendants may override visibility:hidden;
761:display:none and opacity:0 suppress every descendant. content-visibility:auto
762:counts when browser range geometry exists (unrendered skipped content supplies
763:no geometry); content-visibility:hidden excludes. Use computed CSS overflow
764:values, including the browser's visible-to-auto cross-axis conversion. Fixed
765:and sticky descendants use current geometry only, without hypothetical ancestor
766:scroll movement; the clip model is conservative for their special containing
767:blocks and does not claim they can be revealed by scrolling. display:none,
768:visibility:hidden/collapse, opacity:0 and content-visibility:hidden remain
769:excluded, as do editable/input/script text and layoutless text. This does not
770:claim occlusion detection or virtualized content that is not mounted. Existing
771:normalization, smallest matching element and strict ambiguity remain unchanged.
772:
773:### Upgrade and gate presentation
774:
775:Upgrade snapshots count security notes alongside compiler notes and report
776:pending review gates separately, with unavailable previous gate data stated
777:honestly for old snapshots. The gate status reflects acknowledgments without
778:writing them during reporting. A refuted invariant cannot be acknowledged:
779:check explicitly reports that --ack-security was not applied, leaves review
780:records untouched and explains that the policy/intent must be repaired.
781:The JSON field acknowledgmentNotApplied carries the same explanation. The
782:existing Report::refuted predicate determines refutations (LLP 3000.036.004);
783:ordinary advisory notes never fail check. Only check acknowledgment paths
784:write dispositions, and delta reporting only reads them. The CLI takes one
785:--ack-security ID per invocation; when refuted, that entire single-ID write is
786:suppressed and check exits nonzero. Old upgrade CLIs invoke the newly installed
787:check and cannot acknowledge/bypass its gates. The advance scan is bounded
788:heuristic migration advice, never a security gate; indirect aliases or arbitrary
789:use(t) dataflow can be missed. Mixed failures show
790:schema/device remediation before the gate repair/ack command, explicitly
791:subject to repairing any refutation first. Gate-only upgrade failures name
792:check --ack-security <ID> --reason '<spec rationale>'; other failures retain
793:schema/device remediation. An already published old CLI may still print its
794:old E_UPGRADE line; the new check child must carry the actionable explanation.
795:The release card records the 0.4.37 CLI's obsolete closed-door/startDev advice.
796:The numeric-advance candidate scan requires an actual advance call in the same
797:local expression/assignment as numeric consumption; unrelated operators or
798:new Date/assert calls elsewhere in a file do not qualify.
799:
800:### Protected immutable payloads
801:
802:A column-read-protected payload may be immutable. On insert its supplied value
803:is validated normally. On every update/existing-upsert, a supplied protected
804:immutable field is ignored without comparing it with stored data, for every
805:viewer, including its readers and the system. Its retained value and asset
806:placement remain unchanged; no upload ownership or placement check runs for
807:the ignored field because no asset is adopted (amending LLP 3000.036 §22.9).
808:An ignored newly uploaded asset remains unplaced under the existing uploader
809:ownership, accounting and collection lifetime; no extra adoption or extension
810:is created. Immutability constrains update/existing-upsert within one row
811:lifetime; delete is independently authorized by the table delete rule. It
812:does not add an undeletable identity policy or prohibit a newly authorized
813:insert after deletion. The operation still validates supplied input types/bounds; ignoring occurs
814:before merge/placement, including explicit null for an optional field. Invalid
815:operation input still fails using schema/site facts alone. An
816:existing upsert differing only in these fields succeeds with them retained;
817:absent-upsert still inserts. Compiler advice on an
818:explicit patch names that behavior using schema/site facts only. Device
819:prediction retains the old cell or its omission and never predicts the supplied
820:cell. Ordinary jobs/system updates follow the same rule; schema-evolution
821:backfills remain the explicit replacement path. Ordinary unprotected immutability retains
822:its existing contract. Cap/unique/index/authority/derived dependencies on
823:protected fields remain refused. This input-independent policy preserves
824:LLP 3000.036 §22.8's noninterference oracle and needs no new device protocol:
825:the same interpreter runs it on the server and device.
826:
827:### Audience helpers and hidden probes
828:
829:Expression-bodied helpers, or a block containing one return, may appear in
830:table/column rules when expansion is exactly the existing rule grammar.
831:Arguments are ordinary typed rule terms, substituted simultaneously without
832:caller capture. Only parameters, viewer and rule constants may be free in the
833:helper; a helper's focal dot cannot capture the caller's row. Nested helpers
834:are bounded to eight levels and 4096 visited syntax nodes per rule (including
835:arguments and each expanded body), refused as E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR on
836:exhaustion. Direct and indirect cycles are detected on the expansion stack.
837:Parameters cannot shadow viewer or rule constants. exists is explicitly
838:allowed and pays the same probes after expansion/inversion as handwritten
839:rules; ordinary reads are excluded. Simultaneous substitution prevents capture;
840:cycles,
841:wrong arity/types, block effects/lets, arithmetic, clock and ordinary reads
842:refuse with E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR and an actionable repair. No new runtime
843:instruction, probe semantics, authority or resource allowance is introduced.
844:Expansion precedes read-through, index inference, audience inversion and
845:security analysis so every consumer sees the same canonical predicate.
846:
847:Program exists remains a caller-visible read; rule exists retains its existing
848:policy probe semantics. N_UNREADABLE_PROBE and N_LOOKUP_FILTERED name the exact
849:hiding read/read-through rule and source site, including exists directly in a
850:require. No runtime refusal confirms whether a hidden row physically exists.
851:Runnable guide recipes demonstrate protected immutable payloads, a role helper
852:and a hidden parent probe alongside a deliberately minimal readable projection.
853:
854:### Required evidence
855:
856:Regression coverage includes repeated startServe/advance/drain runs on one
857:store, drain:false restart and legacy saved boundaries; no double session
858:shift; production controls and failed-checkpoint atomicity; mounted below-fold
859:RN-web text and hidden/clip controls; gate-only and refuted upgrade diagnostics;
860:real and unrelated advance scan candidates; protected immutable equal/different
861:patches, upserts, assets and server/device observation equality; helper types,
862:recursion, capture and authority equivalence; hidden program exists source
863:advice. Every changed guide snippet executes against the fresh pack. Full
864:five-crate Rust tests, affected client pool, Mac Studio affected platform tests,
865:qualify-linux --consumer-only (test.sh is prohibited), published 0.4.35/0.4.37
866:old-CLI upgrades, refs/docs budget and repository-root checks are recorded in
867:WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. No version bump, publication or push.

FILE llp/3000.000.000.002-declarative-backfills.rfc.md
19:## 2. Language
20:
21:A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
22:simultaneous output patch:
23:
24:```quarry
25:backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
26:  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
27:```
28:
29:Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
30:they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
31:compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
32:The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
33:names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
34:read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
35:disjoint. Names are unique per table. Durable store history keeps one
36:normalized definition per table/name, including gaps; changing it refuses with a
37:bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.
38:
39:The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
40:comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
41:and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
42:`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
43:and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
44:means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
45:Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
46:are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
47:`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
48:and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
49:viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
50:Identity `id` is never an output. Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
51:engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse, including fields
52:owned by a historical source lifetime even when the candidate removes that rule.
53:Restored narrowed rows retain their individual output ownership, captures and
54:reference types. Columns added by later predecessor schemas contribute their
55:own types/defaults only when absent from that archived definition. Overwriting an
56:existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
57:existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.
58:
59:Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
60:spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
61:ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
62:Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
63:for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
64:cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
65:Before evaluating any patch, each unique lookup index validates the historical
66:population's cardinality with bounded privileged duplicate samples. A different
67:simultaneous patch making candidate keys unique cannot choose an arbitrary old
68:match. All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
69:read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
70:remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
71:only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
72:operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
73:cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.
74:
75:## 3. Adoption and reversibility
76:
77:The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:
78:
79:1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
80:   existing restoration intent. This includes narrowed rows eligible to return:
81:   restore their full historical images before evaluation, deferring only the
82:   backfill output columns' candidate-type checks. Retained reference invalidation,
83:   source lifetime and audience checks still apply before restoration. Retire
84:   source-expired lifetimes before any backfill; a transform cannot extend an
85:   expiry or opening boundary.
86:2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
87:   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
88:   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
89:   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
90:   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
91:   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
92:   Membership is per row lifetime, with normalized declaration definitions kept
93:   beside evidence. Removing a whole table suspends its membership rather than
94:   feeding transformed values back as inputs. Re-entering that declaration reads
95:   its original overwritten inputs and preserves its current result; selecting
96:   original source instead reverses it even when the immediately previous schema
97:   lacked the table/declaration. Ordinary active deletion clears membership.
98:3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
99:   carry-forward and reopening run none for ordinary active rows: candidate-native
100:   writes are not historical rows. Newly restored narrowed rows receive carried
101:   declarations too, including a declaration introduced while they were dormant. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
102:   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
103:   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
104:   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
105:   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
106:4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
107:   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
108:   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
109:   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
110:   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
111:   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
112:   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
113:   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
114:   output edit because another input happened to change. A fully reversed
115:   same-column output edited during absence also refuses re-entry when its
116:   current interpreted value equals neither preimage nor saved result, even
117:   with unchanged arguments. Sparse defaults count by interpreted value.
118:5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
119:   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
120:   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
121:   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
122:   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.
123:
124:Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
125:normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
126:It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
127:output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
128:its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
129:preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
130:changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
131:device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
132:metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
133:sets `snapback4-release-store-6`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
134:format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
135:previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged. Prune previews
136:backfill preimages separately, with counts, bytes and bounded ids. Explicit
137:prune deletes their values and keeps only per-lifetime reversal-unavailable
138:identities. Leaving an overwritten column then refuses with a pruned-preimage
139:reason: select a bridge retaining present meaning or restore a backup. Active
140:row deletion clears these identities too. Pruning added-column evidence does
141:not prevent rollback when no old-visible field needs a preimage.
142:
143:Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
144:preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
145:whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
146:and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
147:errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
148:a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
149:repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
150:source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
151:selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
152:meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
153:forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
154:requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
155:does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.
156:
157:`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
158:restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
159:can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
160:text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
161:These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
162:rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
163:different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.
164:
165:Consumed historical references must retain valid parent-lifetime evidence even
166:when the input column is dropped. Expired predecessor/restored images are retired
167:before any transform, including ordinary expiry without read-through. Pruning
168:keeps membership-only tombstones solely for surviving lifetimes; such a tombstone
169:refuses re-entry requiring lost values rather than treating the row as fresh.
170:
171:Re-entry retains an adoption-cohort identity with each active row receipt.
172:All declarations first applied in one activation, including continuously carried
173:declarations on tables that were never removed, read one reconstructed source
174:snapshot, including point/range indexes; this overlays that cohort's complete
175:input evidence and output preimages (including original absence) on current
176:images. Distinct original cohorts are evaluated separately against their
177:respective historical overlays, so a declaration introduced later can read an
178:earlier declaration's already-transformed values. Computed and retained/native output values must fit candidate scalar types and
179:text minimum/format constraints before narrowing; invalid generated outputs
180:refuse the entire adoption with affected-row samples. Patches remain staged until
181:all cohorts succeed, and current row images are restored before applying them.
182:New declarations use the current assembled predecessor snapshot. Inactive
183:cohorts restore their own still-omitted input interpretations, independently of
184:new declarations in that activation, and catch up changed authored inputs. Declared historical arguments consume retained
185:cells and source definitions even when the candidate omits those columns;
186:retained parent-lifetime bindings still apply. A native-image exception requires a birth marker for this exact declaration,
187:a matching fingerprint of the original non-output inputs, and valid, complete outputs;
188:a new declaration never skips valid old inputs merely because an older
189:transformation left a retained output. Receipt reference inputs use private namespaced bindings in the existing
190:dormant parent-lifetime ledger, independently of the live input column. A
191:retargeted/dropped input cannot substitute its current binding for the saved
192:reference. Every unchanged saved argument consumed during active or inactive
193:re-entry validates its private lifetime binding before program evaluation.
194:Bindings use each restored narrowed row's actual archived reference type
195:(rather than the table-wide predecessor), even when an
196:argument is declared as generic `id`. Existing private bindings survive
197:re-entry with unchanged saved inputs even after the visible column becomes
198:generic `id`; schema type erasure cannot recapture the same parent id. Reconstruction validates any consumed saved binding before restoring
199:its value. Original absence is recorded separately from the predecessor's interpreted
200:value. A physically missing field consumes its source default when present,
201:otherwise null; receipts retain that interpretation explicitly, preventing
202:later defaults from changing argument or lookup/index evaluation. Expired sources retire when entering or leaving the
203:last declaration, including restored table images.
204:
205:When transformed rows become dormant, candidate output reference types supply
206:parent bindings even when the predecessor had no such column. Existing invalid
207:bindings are preserved. If a restored row becomes dormant again, only its
208:explicitly backfilled authoritative cells update the retained image; original
209:source definition, other cells and authority provenance stay intact. Forward
210:selection after rollback reconstructs absent added outputs for that same
211:declaration before evaluating its arguments, rather than feeding it its own
212:retained result.
213:
214:Input omission is stored independently from interpreted null/default values,
215:both for the current physical lifetime and for each receipt. Ordinary writes
216:compare interpreted old/new inputs; meaningful edits end omission provenance,
217:while unrelated writes and default materialization do not. Receipts reconstruct
218:their original interpretation of omitted fields across later default changes.
219:
220:Rows inserted while a declaration is active receive a private native-birth
221:marker keyed by its normalized definition and a hash of their input image;
222:no original input values are kept in that marker. The same declaration preserves
223:complete native images with unchanged inputs, including absent optional inputs,
224:constant transforms and whole-table restoration. Changed native inputs, invalid
225:present inputs, lost native output images or conflicting other-transform history
226:refuse with a bridge reason. An inactive overlapping receipt is compatible only
227:when every overlapping current output equals its recorded preimage; an active
228:or pruned conflicting history still refuses. Same-column output values belong
229:to the candidate type, rather than the historical input type, for native rows.
230:A new declaration name does not inherit another
231:name's birth marker. Prune retains only native fingerprints for surviving
232:lifetimes, alongside current omission flags; receipt absence flags are forgotten
233:with historical values. Delete/reset remove every marker for the ended lifetime.
234:
235:Source retirement uses the ordinary engine expiry/reference-action closure
236:under the complete assembled predecessor schema, before any patch runs or
237:removed table is archived. Only surviving post-action rows enter dormant storage. Restored source
238:indexes and cap occupancy are rebuilt first; writing-reference groups must fit
239:the source reservation's live bound. Cascade and set-null effects participate
240:in this same transaction; restrict or retained-child blockers refuse the entire
241:adoption. Retired source population forces a new generation so clients cannot
242:keep children removed by a cascade. Authoritative set-null updates corresponding archived cells/narrowed images
243:to null before clearing old column bindings, while receipt input bindings remain
244:independent. An expired
245:narrowed source is exposed only inside this uncommitted lifecycle phase and
246:cannot reach a backfill evaluation alive.
247:
248:Development reset still empties every lifetime's data/history/native/omission
249:markers, then reconstructs code-derived declaration/input registries from its
250:retained schema before reseeding; the new identity's rows receive new birth
251:markers. No release HTTP reset door is added.
252:
253:Evidence decode is preflighted against
254:the existing job heap ceiling. Text preview lists private preimages separately
255:from dormant application rows/cells/index entries.
256:
257:### Per-column transformed views
258:
259:A receipt records a `retained_outputs` set independently of its declaration's
260:active flag. When leaving a declaration, candidate-visible transformed outputs
261:reverse to their preimages. Outputs absent from the candidate remain transformed
262:in dormant storage; their set membership survives until reversal or re-entry.
263:Even an inactive declaration reverses those suspended fields when a later plain
264:schema exposes them. Whole-table suspension keeps the transformed view intact.
265:
266:Re-entry preserves the saved preimage for each still-transformed output. A
267:fully reversed output instead captures its actual assembled source value,
268:including restored dormant cells and interpreted predecessor defaults, before
269:applying the patch. Input history and rollback preimages are separate: an input
270:may retain its original omitted/null interpretation while the latest rollback
271:view has a literal default. Ordinary writes do not erase omission provenance
272:when they edit a transformed output from that same original cohort.
273:
274:An inverse restoring genuine absence records a transaction-local source mask.
275:Later evaluation cannot fill that absence from the suspended result or a default
276:belonging to the output schema. The mask is dropped on every exit. Earlier
277:recorded cohorts' staged patches are replayed into later recorded source views;
278:fresh declarations still share the untouched predecessor snapshot and cannot
279:read each other's newly staged outputs. Every decode remains heap-admitted.
280:
281:Reference arguments copy any matching ordinary cell-lifetime bindings before
282:restoration consumes that ledger, including transformed reference cells absent
283:from an archived whole-row definition. Unchanged saved inputs retain existing
284:private bindings; inferred current bindings cannot overwrite their invalidation.
285:The stable format is `snapback4-release-store-6`, with schema capability
286:`release-backfills-v3`. Published 0.4.33's store format 3 upgrades on first use.
287:Unpublished experimental formats 4/5 and the older capabilities cannot silently read
288:this evidence layout. Package versions are not changed in this workstream.
289:
595:## 8. Wave-21 release evolution
596:
597:Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever (EVOL), 2026-10-21; target 0.4.37.
598:This amendment supersedes the insert-only input policy in §3. Ordinary
599:writes while the exact declaration is active refresh existing native input
600:fingerprints. They do not create native birth evidence for migrated rows.
601:Active forward receipts refresh only actually edited, currently authored
602:non-output inputs and their reference lifetime bindings. Overwritten historical
603:inputs, original output preimages, omission interpretations on unrelated
604:writes, inactive receipts and immutable definitions retain their custody.
605:An older-release edit does not receive this refresh. At re-entry a changed
606:input is compatible when deterministic recomputation over the assembled source
607:produces the exact stored output; an incompatible edit without accepted native evidence still refuses.
608:Valid native outputs written under the owning declaration remain authored
609:values, even when they differ from the historical transform. Refresh replaces
610:the fingerprint, rather than accumulating old accepted images.
611:
612:Rename destination conflicts consult only physically present or retained cells.
613:An absent optional destination is absent; interpreting it as null or a default
614:cannot manufacture a conflict. Explicit present null is still a real cell.
615:
616:SEC6 owns rule-changing deploy review items, write intent, projection privacy
617:and client session validation; EVOL does not change those contracts.
618:
619:
620:A table declares `table observations renamed from behaviors:`. It moves the
621:same numeric table identity and row/reference lifetimes, never copies rows or
622:merges independent histories. Both names cannot appear together in a selected
623:schema. The durable name relation permits old-source rollback and older-source
624:writes to return under the newer name. Existing independent destination storage
625:refuses atomically. Indexes, retained type definitions, asset homes and migration
626:receipts follow that identity; literal values and operation names do not change.
627:Store format 8 and the release-table-renames-v1 capability fence the new metadata.
628:
629:`rollback --check --bridge DIR` now writes the selected target's complete source,
630:loosening only differing compatible value domains to their union with current
631:source and retaining new authoritative tables with their current rules. It lists
632:changes and direct target read/write operations on affected tables. It never changes immutable
633:source or activates automatically. The generated bridge is a new deployable
634:release, subject to ordinary compilation/adoption. Incompatible nominal types,
635:new business logic, current guards absent from v1, and old UI assumptions about
636:null require explicit review; diagnostics and the app-specific checklist remain
637:visible. A retained new table does not claim a frozen v1 operation exposes it.
638:No reverse projection fabricates an asset, reference, list element or missing
639:table. Scalar `down` remains the answer for a truthful scalar substitution;
640:loosened bridges cover nullable files/images, empty image lists and optional
641:money without invented placeholders.
642:
643:Preview causes are recorded by the actual narrowed-population closure: a column
644:and incompatible value category, or a typed reference to a hidden parent. Counts
645:may overlap across causes and are schema populations before read rules. New-table
646:rows have a separate cause. Foresight consults declared reverse writers rather
647:than calling their old output absent. SEC6 owns review-item resurfacing.
648:
649:Asset columns (`file`, image/video and asset lists) use `renamed from` as an
650:identity move, rather than computed scalar forward/down substitutions. The
651:active and retained physical cells and the ledger's one home move together;
652:selecting old source changes the public name back, preserving current values.
653:Both aliases cannot be active together; a conflicting physical/retained cell
654:refuses atomically. Reference/captured/engine field renames remain excluded.
655:Older runtimes refuse store format 8; device projections omit migration metadata.
656:
657:Cross-table historical existence and unique point reads are first-class
658:backfill inputs under §2's assembled-source, metered system authority. A guide
659:example must include the source index and explicit row input rather than an
660:application reconciliation loop. Outputs remain disjoint scalar patches and
661:all declarations observe the same pre-patch snapshot.
662:
663:`fileText(file)` is a migration-only deterministic input, accepted in backfill
664:and down expressions, including through pure helpers. It reads the immutable
665:retained file named by the ledger, as UTF-8. It never reads an application path
666:or URL, unverified bytes, arbitrary ids, or a device's filesystem. The runtime
667:requires an internal migration context and charges the job heap and step meter
668:before reading at most the ledger byte length plus one. Absent bytes, released
669:assets, length disagreement, invalid UTF-8 or meter exhaustion refuse the whole
670:adoption. Preview reads the same original immutable asset root while writing
671:only its private database snapshot. The feature creates no user-callable
672:migration operation and does not make arbitrary file contents readable to users.
673:`nonemptyLines(text)` counts trimmed nonempty lines, including repeated entries
674:separately; CRLF and whitespace-only lines have their ordinary text meaning.
675:It is a pure scalar transform; an empty textual list counts zero. A generated
676:scalar is reversible through the existing output-preimage/evidence custody;
677:fileText never mutates the bytes or their home. Ordinary new-order byte validation
678:is outside this release-migration primitive; it receives no invented enforcement
679:claim from a historical backfill.
680:
681:
682:### EVOL review clarifications
683:
684:“Exact declaration” means its normalized typed-IR digest, not a release number.
685:A declaration can be carried in later source. “Currently authored” means a
686:column present in that selected schema whose interpreted value actually changes
687:in an application write; hidden inputs and overwritten migration arguments are
688:excluded. “Older-release edit” means a write while this exact declaration is
689:absent. Replacing accepted non-output inputs intentionally forgets their previous
690:live values: this is live data, not a history of every application write. It
691:never forgets an overwritten output's reversal preimage. For migrated rows the
692:parent's dependency/output comparison still applies, with exact recomputation
693:agreement added for both transformed and fully reversed cells. Native rows with
694:matching refreshed non-output fingerprints and valid complete outputs preserve
695:their authored images. A fingerprint mismatch needs full recomputation agreement
696:or refuses; it never authorizes overwriting a different authored image.
697:
698:A helper inherits the migration context transitively when expanded. Calling a
699:helper containing fileText outside a backfill/down refuses during compilation;
700:raw IR also refuses at execution without the unforgeable internal context/root.
701:Failure in a forward migration, down, or exact preview aborts that entire
702:transaction. Preview has the identical job precharge and file validation.
703:File-read failures never print raw bytes. Output scalars are deliberately
704:authored data under their destination rules; an author copying file text into
705:a readable text column must choose its audience explicitly, as for any backfill.
706:No automatic output or diagnostic exposes the input bytes. Asserted missing
707:points refuse the whole operation. Nullable points preserve §2's ordinary
708:nullable semantics only through the author's explicit expression; no missing
709:point becomes an invented preimage. The database snapshot pins ledger and point
710:reads; asset bytes are immutable under their ledger id, read at exact length.
711:
712:Rename conflicts compare a destination cell not owned by the moving alias in
713:the same surviving row lifetime. They do not compare the source cell against
714:itself or consult dead row lifetimes. Table moves keep their numeric identity;
715:independent destination identities cannot be merged. Asset renames never revive
716:a released asset. A selected schema still validates every moved asset against
717:the ledger/type. Reference, captured and engine-field renames are diagnosed
718:before activation, rather than silently losing their bindings.
719:
720:The bridge's compatible joins are: identical underlying types with optionality
721:union; integer bounds with the lower minimum and higher maximum; matching-kind assets
722:with the higher byte limit (file MIME and image-once policy unchanged);
723:same-format text with the lower minimum and higher maximum; enum member
724:union; lists with recursively compatible element types, lower minimum and higher
725:maximum. Other nominal/type conversions are not guessed; they remain target
726:domains, are listed for explicit typed repair, and receive no promise of complete
727:visibility. Existing compiler diagnostics and the checklist expose operations
728:needing adaptation; readers AND writers on affected tables are listed. The
729:bridge never executes a scalar asset projection: it changes only the selected
730:schema's value domain over the same retained actual cells. Old nullable UI
731:assumptions remain an application compatibility check, not a claimed guarantee.
732:It keeps one alias for a renamed table, selecting the target's spelling; the
733:other name remains only in the durable identity relation. That spelling routes
734:old reads/writes to the same numeric row lifetime. It never adds both aliases as
735:independent tables. Existing target rule/intent text remains byte-identical;
736:new tables retain current rule text. Ordinary deploy preview reports current
737:safeguards the target lacks. SEC6 owns rule-review resurfacing; EVOL adds no
738:blocking review gate or invented safety guarantee.
739:
740:A cause is either `(table,column,incompatible-value-category,target-type)` or
741:`(table,column,hidden-parent-table)`. Activation first hides incompatible rows,
742:then iterates typed reference edges until no additional dependent hides. Only rows physically in the current active `r` namespace immediately before
743:activation contribute to these preview counts; already dormant rows do not.
744:Neither source nor target application read rules filter this denominator. A row may have more
745:than one cause, so cause counts are explicitly non-additive; total hidden rows
746:are distinct. New-table rows are counted separately. Static foresight compares
747:possible future domains and declared reverse writers; exact preview runs the
748:actual activation against current data. Both are privileged local operator
749:outputs, never per-user query results or public HTTP metadata. Private preview
750:writes are removed, never published to the live database or generation.
751:
752:
753:The native and migrated re-entry algorithms are distinct. Native acceptance is
754:available only when the row has NO forward evidence receipt for the declaration
755:and has its native marker. A migrated row NEVER gains that marker from an edit.
756:For each migrated row, every re-entry evaluates the patch again against its
757:reconstructed inputs and current point dependencies, regardless of whether saved
758:inputs compare equal. Refreshing its non-output inputs/bindings changes that
759:input basis, not its `after` computed-result baseline or output preimages. If
760:fresh computation differs from saved `after`, a stored output equal to the old
761:computed result, reversed preimage, or fresh computation permits writing the
762:fresh computation, never retaining a stale value; a different manual output
763:conflicts. Successful adoption records the fresh computation as `after`. If inputs and computation are unchanged,
764:the parent's explicit edited-added-cell preservation rule applies. Thus a live
765:retarget P1 → P2 with O=f(P1) never skips computation at re-entry: it either
766:recomputes to f(P2) or refuses a conflicting edited O. Backfill is an activation
767:transform, not steady-state maintained-data enforcement. A native image whose
768:current input fingerprint matches its accepted native marker instead preserves
769:its complete valid authored outputs; a mismatching native image needs exact full
770:recomputation agreement or refuses. These branches cannot substitute for each
771:other.
772:
773:Bridge operation listing covers direct lowered Quarry read/write table sites,
774:including expanded pure helpers. It is a conservative table-level diagnostic,
775:not a complete dependency proof: indirect rule dependencies, actions, external
776:workers and frozen UI assumptions require the checklist's manual workflow audit.
777:The lack of a complete-visibility promise for incompatible type conversions says
778:nothing about the separate operation-listing diagnostic. No listing grants
779:authority to deploy or replaces ordinary compiler/adoption validation.
780:
781:For avoidance of doubt, “active” in visibility accounting is a storage state,
782:not a viewer's source-rule result. With 1,000 physical active rows and 400 denied
783:to some viewer, all 1,000 are in the privileged preview denominator. With 600
784:physical active rows and 400 already dormant, only 600 are in that denominator.
785:If the target hides 100 of those 600, `hiddenRows` is 100. No source/target rule
786:predicate is executed to convert that into a per-user number. This preserves the
787:parent DORM contract and cannot leak to an app caller: the tool is local operator
788:inspection only.
789:
790:
791:In the migrated branch, `deps_same` means BOTH input values and fresh computed
792:patch equal their saved counterparts. If inputs change but the patch equals
793:saved `after`, `deps_same` is false: the fresh patch is written when the current
794:output equals saved `after`, its preimage, or that same fresh patch; any other
795:manual value refuses. Matching more than one allowed baseline has no precedence
796:ambiguity: each allows writing the same single fresh patch. Only `deps_same`
797:plus a still-transformed, present output invokes the parent's explicit preservation
798:of edited added cells. Every successful staged receipt records fresh inputs and
799:fresh `after`, retaining the correct predecessor preimages.
800:
801:If both a native marker and a forward receipt for the same digest are present,
802:the receipt takes precedence: evaluate the migrated algorithm and its exact
803:baseline/preimage custody. The marker cannot skip that computation. This overlap
804:is not produced by ordinary writes (which never create a native marker on a
805:migrated row), but precedence is explicit for retained/legacy state. Pruned
806:membership is checked first and cannot become native acceptance. Down receipts
807:have distinct down digests and do not establish native-forward status.
808:
809:“Affected” for bridge listing includes domain-loosened tables, retained new
810:authoritative tables, and identities whose table/asset-column spelling moves.
811:Use the target spelling for a renamed identity, with its current spelling noted
812:as provenance; never list the aliases as two independent tables. Emit direct
813:lowered Quarry readers/writers on those tables. The separate manual audit still
814:covers indirect rule/action/worker/UI dependencies.
815:
816:
817:### EVOL readiness record
818:
819:The exact round-6 amendment received independent [Muse READY](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-evol-r6.muse.txt)
820:and [fresh Codex READY](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-evol-r6.codex.txt).
821:The [brief and provenance](./reviews/3000.000.000.002-evol-r6.provenance.md)
822:retain the same semantic text, commands and digest. This is RFC readiness, not
823:author acceptance or a publication claim. Linux/Mac regression and copied-lane
824:qualification results are recorded in WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md.
825:
826:## 9. Edited migrated outputs (ROLL)
827:
828:Commissioned for 0.4.39, 2026-10-22. LLP 3000.000.000 §20 and its review
829:clarifications govern default rollback views and the closed safety boundary.
830:An in-place output edited under its declaring source may use a declared down
831:on its current assembled image, or keep the exact value in a target domain
832:which already admits it. A failing old type/constraint still refuses; no
833:per-row widening or invented scalar is allowed. Unedited outputs keep exact
834:preimage precedence, with a receipt saying when that old view is stale until
835:forward. Real forward conflicts include competing values and bridge repair.
836:
837:Receipts distinguish the original preimage, authored newer image, saved
838:computation, selected old physical image and presence. Equal explicit inputs
839:alone cannot authorize retaining an edited output: fresh point/existence
840:recomputation must also agree with the saved computation, or exactly equal
841:the actual authored image. Changed projected cells, parents, pruned evidence
842:and meter cuts still refuse atomically. Editing never creates native-birth
843:status. Format 9 fences this retained law, including after selecting old source;
844:format 8 remains readable and upgrades transactionally on successful adoption.
845:Repeated cycles, partially retired outputs and table-name changes preserve
846:that evidence. The implementation adds focused regressions for those paths.
847:
848:
849:## 10. Wave-25 day-two friction (DX8)
850:
851:Implementer: Codex / Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-23. Commission: wave-25 fix list
852:8b #1–#13, building on DX7 and Integration 23/24. This amendment is a reviewed
853:implementation commission, not author acceptance or a publication claim.
854:The no-JavaScript migration, atomic activation, job-meter and row-lifetime
855:custody laws above still apply. Integration 23 owns rollback-view selection and
856:its trigger; SEC9 owns column-write authority, security gates and adversarial
857:campaign reach. DX8 changes neither of those lanes' policy.
858:
859:### Migration interpretation and current-value renames
860:
861:Backfill arguments and lookups read the assembled pre-patch snapshot, before
862:any simultaneous rename/backfill writes outputs. Historical argument names
863:therefore name predecessor fields. A declaration's immutable meaning is its
864:normalized typed-IR digest. Every changed-meaning refusal, both continuously
865:carried and after a historical gap, prints old and new full digests. A new name
866:is a new declaration; pruning does not reset names or immutable definitions.
867:Prune previews name known down projections whose native/reverse or overlapping
868:forward-preimages they discard. This warning is privileged operator output;
869:prune remains explicit and destructive, and unavailable evidence still refuses
870:subsequent reversal/re-entry. Preview never mutates live history.
871:
872:For the generated `$rename:down` with identical declared old/new scalar types,
873:the current renamed cell outranks its non-null retained predecessor cell. This
874:is an identity spelling change, not a historical computation: v1 must see the
875:edited v2 quote, not the original quote. Stage it on the existing newer snapshot
876:and preserve native/reverse evidence in the same transaction. Repeated
877:rollback/forward cycles retain that current value. Different-type renames still
878:refuse even when sample values happen to fit. Authored down projections retain
879:their exact forward-preimage/non-null-native precedence; a generated rename
880:does not grant another declaration permission to replace protected cells.
881:Conflicting edits under an older view still require an explicit bridge where
882:existing receipt custody cannot reconcile them. The change stays isolated in
883:backfills.rs; it does not alter rollback views or release bridges.
884:
885:### One child per list element
886:
887:`extract CHILD from PARENT via REF each: {destination:sourceList}` extends the
888:existing one-child extraction. Exactly one mapped destination is a required
889:scalar image/file/video; its historical source is a bounded list with an
890:identical element type (optionally a nullable whole list). Other mapped fields
891:retain the existing immutable authoritative scalar-copy law. Empty lists create
892:zero children. A null whole list remains null after an unchanged empty cycle;
893:a populated child set becomes a real list. Duplicate asset elements refuse:
894:one asset cannot acquire two child homes. Unsupported types refuse, never copy
895:or reupload bytes. Default `extract` keeps its existing whole-cell behavior.
896:
897:Per-parent element receipts bind current asset IDs to stable child lifetimes
898:and ordinal positions; new historical elements get deterministic child IDs,
899:independent destination history refuses. Current child asset edits keep their
900:child ID. Reverse selection gathers every extant child, including native v2
901:children with valid captured parent-lifetime evidence, preserving receipt order
902:and appending native children in ID order. It moves current homes back into the
903:old list and records the current element-to-child map. Returning forward follows
904:that map, so old-client reordering preserves child identity and appending a new
905:asset creates one new child. Removed elements mark their receipt-owned child asset absent (an internal null),
906:leaving the retained child outside the active population under ordinary narrowing; they do not
907:silently regain an asset or get deleted. A target list that cannot represent
908:the full current population follows ordinary narrowing, never truncates it.
909:Every receipt-owned child and its parent-lifetime evidence is validated before
910:either forward or reverse processing, including removed elements and intact
911:dormant nulls. A missing/pruned receipt-owned child or invalid parent lifetime refuses rather
912:than recreating history or recapturing a reused parent ID. Reverse selection
913:refuses atomically if a populated child asset home remains in narrowed storage;
914:it names the retained population and requires a loosened child-domain bridge
915:that restores those rows before selecting old source. Migration-owned null
916:removals have no live child asset home and stay dormant across repeated cycles.
917:
918:A successfully applied element extraction records durable definition custody,
919:including an empty initial population. Temporarily retiring its child table or
920:declaration suspends that law when the selected parent no longer declares the
921:source list. Re-entry with the same definition resumes current child rows and
922:homes rather than demanding or replaying the consumed list. It validates every
923:receipt and every restored/current child's existing parent lifetime, captured
924:authority, immutable scalar copies, ledger home and bytes; it never retroactively
925:captures independent children born while the declaration was absent. Missing
926:source plus unknown definition refuses. New parents created while the child
927:table is retired can have zero children. Ordinary narrowed storage remains
928:retained; no row or home is synthesized. Resuming, as every activation, is atomic
929:and metered with the child page and parent image sharing its job allowance.
930:
931:Source/destination read authority and column gates retain the existing strict
932:extraction checks in both directions, including captured parent-authority agreement. One extraction owns each child table, avoiding competing sources for native children. No user migration endpoint or broader
933:asset audience is introduced. Forward, reverse and resumed traversal share the
934:activation page's job meter with every child decode and retained scratch image.
935:The still-resident serialized page and accumulated children consume one allowance;
936:length preflight reserves before fetching/decoding, and only dropped scratch is
937:released. Per-parent traversal/allocation/writes use job meters; a cut aborts the
938:entire activation. Home transfers, row changes,
939:receipts, ordinary reference/narrowing/uniqueness/cap validation and generation
940:publication share the existing writer transaction. Successful adoption fences
941:this law with release-store format 10, including after selecting old source;
942:formats 8/9 remain readable and failed adoption does not upgrade their marker.
943:No successful later adoption downgrades the marker. Serialized schemas carrying
944:`each` also require `release-child-extraction-each-v1`, so older engines refuse
945:the new interpretation before accepting a compiled schema.
946:
947:Hot adoption additionally requires the exact `activation_protocol` token
948:`snapback4-activation-10` on every activation request, including selecting an
949:original saved source with `rollback --exact`. There is no default or legacy
950:fallback. Pre-DX8 hosts reject the unknown required field through their strict
951:command decoder before admission; updated hosts reject missing tokens during
952:decoding and wrong tokens before compiling or adopting a source. The transport
953:fence applies even to a format-9 store and a schema without `each`: a new CLI's
954:rename preview cannot be executed by an older host with stale-preimage rename
955:semantics. The on-disk marker alone cannot fence a running older engine that
956:would never write it. Refusal leaves release, generation, data and format intact;
957:the operator must restart the host with the updated engine before retrying.
958:
959:### Operator processes, development clock and real accounts
960:
961:`snapback4 stop <deployment-dir>` uses the existing same-owner authenticated
962:local release-control channel, then awaits the kernel lock-release barrier.
963:Already stopped is success. An unverifiable/live foreign owner refuses: never
964:unlink a lock or signal a guessed PID. Proof hosts/frontends/tool commands own
965:an explicit process group, including npm wrappers and children. Stop, failure
966:and parent exit release that entire owned group through live keeper/pipe
967:custody; cleanup is bounded and cannot silently leave a serving descendant.
968:Frontend keeper-backed groups are registered for group-drain checks too. If a
969:keeper is lost while a member survives, stop/startup cleanup must report an
970:explicit bounded cleanup failure, even when the host handle has already exited.
971:Processes deliberately leaving custody are outside that promise. Scripts whose
972:stdout is one JSON line use `npm run --silent`; logs remain separate.
973:Changelog/help qualification treats `run` immediately following npm/bun/pnpm
974:as that package manager’s subcommand; later explicit Snapback invocations
975:on the same line still advertise flags and remain checked.
976:
977:A password-authenticated development host exposes clock status/advance with
978:fixture/persona doors closed. It requires an exact full `service:operator`
979:bearer token minted through the existing local store authority. Ordinary users,
980:other seats, read-scoped sessions, unauthenticated requests and production
981:hosts cannot use it. Staging keeps its existing full-service-seat authorization;
982:explicit unsafe development and guest-schema development retain their existing local fixture behavior.
983:Authenticated development jumps preserve live full/read session families and
984:worker leases by default in the same store transaction as the jump's durable
985:authority effects; expired/revoked families never revive. Explicit
986:`sessions: 'expire'` requests expiry testing. Header/SDK reconciliation keeps
987:current clients alive across the jump. The development offset is process-local
988:and returns to wall time on restart; staging retains DX7's persistent checkpoint
989:and paused-drain behavior. A failed/ambiguous jump is never automatically
990:replayed. `startDev` exposes operator-backed now/advance/drain without opening
991:fixture doors. Production uses wall time and refuses clock control.
992:
993:`provisionAccounts` shares seedAccounts plan validation, generated credentials,
994:identity retry/deadline behavior, result shape, selected seat and provision
995:mutation. It accepts production through real signup/login and
996:ordinary authorized mutations; it refuses development and uses no seed/system
997:bypass; staging continues to use seedAccounts. Custom per-user args retain seedAccounts' existing merge precedence.
998:A minted service seat is only that exact principal, not a superuser. The served schema exposes `service_identities`, a list of explicit positive
999:viewer/service equality facts and declared route/schedule seats, excluding result
1000:and enum literals. Both local CLI minting (live or stopped) and the production
1001:helper warn on stderr when a seat has no matching explicit authorization identity.
1002:This is conservative diagnostic evidence, never a proof of admission or denial;
1003:public rules and dynamic application roles still decide actual authority. The mutation's actual read and write rules decide.
1004:
1005:### Inspection and saved artifacts
1006:
1007:Default active sql/data/releases announce the retained dormant row count when
1008:nonzero. Missing active-table errors identify a retained table and its row count
1009:and name a concrete dormant inspection command. Inspection remains a privileged
1010:read-only consistent snapshot, outside application rules. Only direct,
1011:schema-proven media cells in `sql --json-objects` decode to their ordinary wire
1012:JSON values; positional `--json` remains compatible. Truncation and dormancy
1013:notices go to stderr, preserving JSON stdout; existing limit/more fields stay.
1014:
1015:An identical-source deploy with --save-version can refresh an existing saved
1016:version only if the complete Quarry source set and exact bytes match. Differing
1017:source refuses. Generated client artifacts are emitted from that saved source's
1018:Bound, independently of the working tree; fixtures/source evidence stay intact.
1019:Refresh follows successful adoption/no-op and records the correct retained
1020:release identity. Replaying an old source never silently emits the current API.
1021:
1022:### Paging, proof assertions and contract advice
1023:
1024:Board/roster starters and list recipes use newest-first `last N by .id`, while
1025:explicit oldest-first examples remain labelled. The language's ordering default
1026:does not change. Cursors are opaque evaluator-issued positions: send next back
1027:unchanged; a row id/projection is not a cursor. `exhaustPages(readPage)` begins
1028:with null, gathers array pages, follows next, and refuses denial/loading,
1029:malformed/repeated cursors or its explicit page bound. It never turns a capped
1030:or partial read into complete evidence. Projections change fields, not cursor
1031:meaning/order/completeness.
1032:
1033:Day-two recipes compare by stable id across stages and declared common fields,
1034:not list position or whole differently-shaped row images. IDs created by a
1035:frozen v1-client replay are recorded and excluded from preservation baselines.
1036:V2-native IDs may be absent from an exact v1 view; they must reappear unchanged
1037:on forward. Domain-specific synthesized old values need an authored projection
1038:assertion, not an equality claim for incompatible representations.
1039:
1040:renderedText(..., null) equals omitted options. Whitespace normalization,
1041:case-sensitive exact matching by default, explicit substring matching and DX7's
1042:legal scroll reachability remain unchanged. The lane-031 full-line regression
1043:must match mounted reachable `Pump logged.` text, without widening hidden clips.
1044:Write-authority hints explain that a mutation require and the caller's table
1045:rule are separate gates. The captured-vs-live-parent card describes existing
1046:lifetime/audience semantics; it grants no new authority.
1047:
1048:Old-client INPUT BREAK advice follows accepted wire identities, including
1049:references through declared table renames and lowered aliases/defaults. Real
1050:removed/required/narrowed inputs still warn. Pure object-result field additions
1051:are RESULT NOTE, not RESULT REVIEW or an adoption hazard; changed/removed fields
1052:and domains retain review. These remain conservative advice, not a proof of
1053:unchanged business behavior. No runtime input validation is weakened.
1054:
1055:### Required evidence
1056:
1057:Tests cover edited historical/native renames over repeated cycles, identical
1058:and incompatible types, authored-down precedence, continuous/gapped digest
1059:refusals and read-only prune warnings; authenticated dev clock with fixture
1060:doors closed, real SDK sessions and expiry mode; graceful/foreign/idempotent
1061:stop and npm descendant cleanup; production real-account provisioning and seat
1062:warnings; media/truncation/dormancy inspection; saved-source client refresh;
1063:additive results and renamed-reference input advice; list fan-out over edited/native children, old-client reorder/append, empty/null lists, invalid lifetimes, duplicate homes, pruning and atomic refusals; paging progress/failures,
1064:null/full-line rendering and newest-first starter execution. Every changed
1065:guide/starter snippet runs as written against fresh packed artifacts.

1069:client, difftest, packed Linux, wasm/docs/reference and root guide/inventory
1070:checks. Report original failures and scoped reruns independently. Mac is skipped
