llp/3000.000.000-retained-storage-and-hot-release.rfc.md
876:## 19. Dormant visibility and migration (DORM)
877:
878:Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-19; target 0.4.35. This is a
879:proposal until its implementation and two independent READY reviews are
880:recorded. It amends §9.1's prohibition on value substitution only for an
881:explicit, previewed, reversible projection. No inferred coercion is added.
882:The wave-19 day-two evidence distinguishes 21 affected old-client records
883:from retained facts in ten newer-only tables; no row deletion was found.
884:
885:### 19.1 Declared old views and renames
886:
887:Wave-21 EVOL extends this initial surface in [LLP 3000.000.000.002 §8](./3000.000.000.002-declarative-backfills.rfc.md#8-wave-21-release-evolution): active-input refresh, truthful causes, loosened-target bridges, table/asset identity renames and bounded file-text inputs supersede the corresponding initial restrictions below. SEC6 owns security-review resurfacing.
888:
889:A reverse projection belongs to the newer source and runs when that
890:declaration is left. It reads the assembled newer source under activation's
891:system authority and ordinary job meters, before projection or narrowed
892:visibility closure can hide its inputs. Typed inputs and deterministic point
893:reads follow LLP 3000.000.000.002; simultaneous projections read one snapshot
894:and cannot consume each other's new results. A reverse-only declaration is
895:first-class: moving a legacy field to a related table must not require a
896:dummy forward transformation. Output fields are validated against the
897:selected target schema. Identity, reference lifetime, captured authority,
898:asset and engine-owned fields retain their existing safety boundaries.
899:
900:The spelling is `down extensions legacyReason(id: id):` followed by
901:`{reason: (explanations[extension = id] ! else MISSING_REASON).explanation}`.
902:Typed inputs describe the current newer row; outputs describe the selected
903:older view, so an old output field need not remain in the newer table.
904:`explanations.extension` must have a unique point index. A declaration is
905:self-contained and immutable by table/name across retained history.
906:Point-read syntax is inherited from LLP 3000.000.000.002 §2: a bare unique
907:`explanations[extension = id]` returns a nullable row, not a scalar field
908:or a silent field fallback. Field access requires the normal non-null
909:proof or `! else CODE`. An explicitly nullable scalar projection can use
910:`{reason: (explanations[extension = id] ! else MISSING_REASON).explanation
911:if exists explanations where .extension = id else null}`. The existence
912:probe is metered on the same source snapshot; its cut refuses instead of
913:selecting the null branch. No new nullable lookup syntax is introduced.
914:This version has no per-release selector for a down declaration: applicability
915:is solely whether the selected same table has the output field. Two writers
916:for that table/field therefore co-apply whenever either can apply; different
917:intended release numbers do not disambiguate them. Reverse output sets on
918:a table are disjoint, including implicit rename writers. Reject overlapping declarations before any adoption work, naming
919:the declarations and field; types do not establish target applicability.
920:Each surviving target cell has at most one reverse writer after saved
921:forward-preimage/non-null-retained precedence. A declaration applies only when the selected target
922:contains its named output; absent target outputs remain retained, not
923:physically introduced into that release.
924:Both raw-schema admission and every activation recheck this condition for
925:the actual candidate/target pair. Computed down outputs are scalar old-view
926:cells only: no reference, asset, collection, identity, captured/maintained,
927:engine-owned or source lifetime output is admitted. A column rename may
928:only move existing lifetime evidence unchanged; it cannot create a binding.
929:
930:The saved native preimage has the same scalar restriction as the old-view
931:output. A scalar substitution must not archive a newer reference, asset,
932:collection or lifetime-owned cell without custody. A separate scalar column
933:or typed bridge is the repair. Typed reference inputs keep private parent
934:lifetime bindings even when their argument is annotated `id` or an older
935:schema exposes an ordinary id; deleting/reusing that parent cannot authorize
936:recapture on forward or on the next rollback with unchanged inputs. Raw schema
937:validation applies down admission, including unique names, disjoint outputs
938:and sequence ordinals as engine-owned cells. Existence fragments require a
939:declared plain index and a valid prefix/range shape at raw admission;
940:malformed source lookups refuse rather than panic. Every evaluated point/existence lookup sees indexes
941:rebuilt for the assembled source snapshot, including restored rows when no
942:expiry is due. A declaration with no applicable projected cells does not
943:rebuild or validate indexes solely for an expression that will not run.
944:
945:Rows with saved forward-backfill predecessor preimages use those exact
946:preimages. An entering inactive down receipt likewise restores its exact native
947:cells ahead of another leaving declaration's substitution. Non-null native
948:legacy cells also win over a standalone `down`.
949:A retained value synthesized by an earlier `down` remains owned by its
950:receipt, not a native predecessor preimage. On each later rollback it must
951:be recomputed from current declared newer inputs (including changed point
952:dependencies), with a fresh P receipt. A prior synthesized image never
953:suppresses that recomputation or becomes current truth by precedence.
954:The old synthesized image must still match its receipt before replacement;
955:a conflicting edit aborts rather than silently keeping a stale image.
956:A row whose native restoration never completed still owns its inactive
957:receipt: leaving another narrowed view preserves that receipt and projected
958:image rather than recording its projection as a fresh native preimage.
959:A standalone `down` explicitly authorizes replacing missing/null old-view
960:cells, including an intentionally authored null. This is a declared old-view
961:policy rather than an inference that null is missing: an operator who needs
962:an intentionally null old view must author a nullable expression preserving
963:it. Every physical pre-projection value, including null, is retained as the
964:exact newer-cell preimage in P; physical absence has a distinct marker.
965:Forward restores that original null/absence exactly, so synthesis does not
966:discard its authoritative preimage. No provenance claim distinguishes
967:intentional null from historical manufactured null. Thus nulls
968:materialized and retained by earlier old-source rollback do not suppress it.
969:This is declared synthesis, not a claim about a legacy row's birth release.
970:An asserted lookup for an intentionally null old cell can therefore refuse;
971:authors use an explicitly nullable expression when null is the truthful
972:old view and newer source data is absent. Rows born
973:with the newer representation may use the declared reverse projection.
974:Retain the exact newer projected cells and their reverse result in the same
975:transaction. Compatible forward restores those cells rather than deriving
976:an approximation of their authored value. Unrelated edits under the older
977:source survive. A changed projected cell or a changed dependency that would
978:make its restoration ambiguous refuses atomically with a bridge repair.
979:Deletion/expiry ends the lifetime and clears the corresponding evidence;
980:id reuse never inherits it. Prune previews these private images and leaves
981:reversal-unavailable identities when values are explicitly discarded.
982:Missing lookups, meter cuts and invalid reference/authority evidence refuse,
983:never become false absence. A value with no truthful old representation
984:retains ordinary narrowed dormancy; zero observations do not silently become
985:one observation. The declaration is not a claim that different product
986:meanings are equivalent. All indexes, constraints, read-through validation,
987:maintained data, generation and release selection commit with the projection.
988:
989:The outcomes are disjoint. A saved forward predecessor preimage wins. Otherwise an
990:eligible row evaluates the declared expression: an asserted missing point
991:executes its authored refusal and aborts activation; an ordinary nullable
992:point may return null only where the expression explicitly allows it.
993:There is no implicit missing-lookup fallback. A successfully computed value
994:that does not fit the old type follows narrowed dormancy. Invalid/corrupt
995:evidence and authority/lifetime failures abort. A meter cut aborts the entire
996:transaction with the ordinary bounded-work refusal; retrying unchanged
997:inputs is not promised to succeed, and an indexed bridge is the repair.
998:Exact retained native cells win on re-entry; an older-source edit to their
999:declared dependencies that conflicts with that native image refuses rather
1000:than deriving over it. An out-of-domain older dependency edit is still a
1001:conflict; hiding that row under the narrower newer type does not authorize
1002:recapture. Pruned evidence requiring discarded values refuses
1003:explicitly, including in later previews; it never fabricates null/default.
1004:Carrying an active pruned declaration through an unrelated schema change
1005:does not need discarded values while its native cells remain available.
1006:Reintroducing a retired native cell still requires actual retained evidence.
1007:Dependency verification, projection and native eligibility marking also visit
1008:already narrowed row images. Adding a declaration while a native row is
1009:hidden must not skip its later projection. Retired inputs use their retained
1010:source definition, including the default interpretation of physical absence.
1011:Hiding a row or retiring an input column cannot bypass a conflict.
1012:For each row, let P be exactly the applicable fields computed by down after
1013:preimage precedence, including outputs which fail the old target domain
1014:and therefore make the row dormant. Their native preimages and computed
1015:results remain receipt-owned even while the row is narrowed. Re-entry
1016:assembles that retained projected row, verifies the same receipt, restores
1017:its native P cells, then applies destination narrowing/validation. It must
1018:not discard P merely because its old-view value is not visible. Its receipt stores each field's newer value or physical
1019:absence and its old-view result. Forward first verifies every field in P
1020:still has exactly the physical presence and value of that old-view result
1021:(absent, present-null and present-default are distinct; interpretation
1022:through a default cannot make them equal),
1023:then restores only P. Receipt-owned physical absence survives later
1024:narrowing, column retirement and whole-row restoration; an older literal
1025:default must not become its native value. Candidate validation interprets
1026:that absence through the native destination definition while the retained
1027:image keeps it physically absent. Individual dormant cells and whole-row
1028:archives must agree on each receipt-owned patch/inverse before either can
1029:be overlaid into a source snapshot. Fields outside P keep the current
1030:old-source values.
1031:Any conflicting field aborts the entire activation; a disjoint field edit
1032:does not authorize overwriting a conflicting projected field.
1033:
1034:A column rename is declared, never inferred from similar names or types.
1035:The spelling `newName: Type renamed from oldName` names its old column;
1036:both directions preserve the
1037:authoritative cell and lifetime evidence through the same reversible
1038:adoption. A conflicting already-present target value refuses rather than
1039:choosing a winner. Required fields do not acquire invented defaults. Client
1040:argument compatibility is distinct from result shape compatibility: an old
1041:query's result field cannot be promised merely because stored data moved.
1042:Old operation wrappers/projections and the compatibility report must reflect
1043:the actual exposed result shape. Devices execute no migration metadata;
1044:semantic capability/store fences refuse runtimes that cannot interpret the
1045:new declarations or retained evidence.
1046:
1047:The rename is strictly within the same table and row lifetime; its grammar
1048:names a column, never another table/row/parent. Cross-parent and cross-table
1049:moves are not renames. Identical cell types means the full structural type
1050:including numeric/text bounds, enum members, nullability and nominal target,
1051:not merely its base kind. Existing default/constraint validation still
1052:applies to the selected schema; the rename never invents a missing cell
1053:from a new default, truncates or coerces it. A type conversion needs
1054:a separately declared transformation; the rename itself never converts.
1055:The paired rename form uses the same closed scalar/lifetime boundary as
1056:computed down: references, assets, collections and engine-owned/maintained
1057:columns reject during compilation, before either direction can activate.
1058:This initial surface never accepts a forward-only protected rename.
1059:A missing required source cell without saved evidence/default is E_ADOPT,
1060:not an invented optional value. Old and new target schemas validate their
1061:respective view in each direction.
1062:
1063:### 19.2 Forward and captured read-through authority
1064:
1065:Selecting a newer retained source after older-source writes re-enters its
1066:declared backfills. Newly written old-source rows receive required outputs;
1067:changed old-source inputs or lookup dependencies catch up under the existing
1068:cohort/preimage conflict rules. Native newer outputs remain authored values.
1069:This is not a continuously running trigger and does not synthesize a missing
1070:application side-table fanout job. A schema that needs such reconciliation
1071:must declare/prove it or retain a compatible bridge; it cannot claim required
1072:labels have been supplied merely because retained rows returned.
1073:
1074:Changing only a read predicate, including narrowing it then selecting the
1075:older predicate, must not itself make captured read-through rollback refuse.
1076:The reference identity, parent lifetime and authority provenance remain
1077:bound. Reusing another parent, resurrecting an expired capture, or changing
1078:capture structure does not qualify as a predicate-only change. Preserve old
1079:captures; do not recapture a broader grant as a side effect of rollback.
1080:Actual target read rules still authorize every read and the preview warns
1081:when selecting older rules restores broader confidentiality exposure.
1082:The operator is the existing same-UID filesystem owner, already capable of
1083:editing the deployment database directly; activation is not a lesser data
1084:role. This amendment adds no new capability/confirmation for selecting old
1085:rules. Predicate-only means unchanged reference/capture structure and
1086:bound parent lifetime/provenance; only the parent read predicate differs.
1087:Writing the down declaration explicitly authorizes its old-view synthesis;
1088:it is not inferred from a type match. Previews list transformed reverse
1089:cells/rows separately from restoration and display lost confidentiality
1090:clauses. Target rules are the selected source's rules: choosing old source
1091:can intentionally broaden access, as before this amendment. The author's
1092:commission does not introduce a second acknowledgment flag or an audience
1093:containment proof for an explicitly authored operator migration.
1094:
1095:### 19.3 Exact visibility before mechanical diffs
1096:
1097:Rollback preview leads with `N records visible under release C will be
1098:hidden from release T users`. Count only before-active authoritative rows
1099:that become retained dormant in a table T has; show each table and bounded
1100:ids in JSON. Separately count surviving target-visible cells that become
1101:null/default or newly lack a retained legacy cell, and rows in tables T
1102:does not have. The empty-cell category separates newly empty/defaulted cells
1103:from restored empty legacy cells; the latter are not claimed as lost text.
1104:Legacy stores cannot distinguish intentionally null old cells from nulls
1105:materialized by an earlier rollback. Report the exact view without inventing
1106:that provenance. This category counts cells, not distinct records across columns.
1107:Actual expiry/deletion and rebuilt private caches are excluded from dormant
1108:loss. Counts precede read rules and are table populations, never a promise
1109:about a particular viewer's query results. Legacy stores lack reliable
1110:row-birth release provenance, so the preview says `visible under`, not
1111:`created under`. An old column newly shown as null is a factual view change;
1112:the tool does not infer which related business text the application wanted.
1113:Hidden-row counts and surviving-cell counts are disjoint by row presence;
1114:newer-only rows have a table absent from T and cannot also be old-table
1115:losses. No jobs or expiry run on dormant rows (§4). On reactivation the
1116:original source deadline still applies: an expired lifetime can retire then,
1117:and retention never promises that expired records revive indefinitely.
1118:
1119:Each affected class names reverse projection or an expand/contract bridge
1120:as the repair. Rule/guard/operation diffs follow it. Deploy preflight also
1121:warns before future writes to new tables, widened domains or missing old
1122:fields can make a later rollback hide rows or show empty/defaulted cells.
1123:For these three syntactic risk classes the warning is conservative: no
1124:false negatives for new tables, widened value domains and removed old fields;
1125:a fitting authored projection may make a warning a false positive.
1126:Exact reverse adoption on the post-deploy snapshot reports current effects
1127:separately from conservative future-write risks. Previews use MAIN tables
1128:in their private disk snapshot: read-through's connection-local temp-store
1129:configuration cannot erase their before image. All scratch disappears on
1130:success or refusal. Nothing changes live rows, pointers, history or time.
1131:
1132:### 19.4 Operator inspection and retained identity
1133:
1134:`sql --dormant --release DIR` and `sql --all-releases --release DIR` are
1135:mutually exclusive read-only operator views under one pinned SQLite read
1136:snapshot. The first contains retained whole/narrowed rows and retained cells;
1137:the second includes active and retained images. Removed table names and
1138:retained column names are queryable through generated logical application
1139:relations; internal namespaces remain unavailable to submitted SQL. Factual
1140:state/source metadata distinguish active rows, dormant rows and dormant
1141:cells. A retained-cell record contains its id and retained cells, with
1142:unprovided columns null. Conflicting images are separate labelled records,
1143:never an arbitrary winning value. Inspection-mode reserved-name collisions
1144:refuse instead of shadowing application columns. These modes bypass read
1145:rules as existing owner-only SQL does; they add no HTTP/client namespace.
1146:`all-releases` means every image still retained, not time-travel snapshots
1147:or one invented version of every row per historical release.
1148:`_snapback_columns` is the JSON array of actually provided application cells
1149:on each inspection record, so a retained null is distinguishable from an
1150:unprovided SQL NULL. This is provenance, not a missing-value substitution.
1151:
1152:Equivalent retained executable source remains selectable across compiler
1153:upgrades. Compiler reports or equivalent old/new packaging metadata do not
1154:change source identity; actual source, native and worker behavior remain
1155:identity-bearing and full control-request integrity still covers all files.
1156:Ordinary refused deploys leave neither numbered candidates nor stages.
1157:This is a source-bundle identity, not a frozen compiler-binary identity or
1158:a proof of behavior equivalence between compiler versions. Activation
1159:recompiles retained source with the selected runtime and revalidates every
1160:data/authority constraint. Actual compiler semantic fixes can change
1161:execution without minting a new source id; version negotiation and the
1162:normal upgrade/publication qualification remain independent fences.
1163:Backfill/reverse definition identity still includes its normalized typed
1164:IR. Recompilation that changes that historical definition cannot reuse its
1165:old receipts: immutable-name checks refuse and require an explicit new-name
1166:bridge. Selecting the source id across an upgrade is supported; success of
1167:an incompatible historical migration after an arbitrary semantic compiler
1168:change is not promised. Removing the source-identity trap does not bypass
1169:these admission checks.
1170:
1171:### 19.5 Argument aliases and review evidence
1172:
1173:`explanation: text 1..500 alias reason` declares one canonical argument and
1174:one accepted old spelling on a Quarry query/mutation. Normalize the old
1175:spelling to the canonical value before typed execution, including local
1176:device execution. Both spellings supplied at once refuse E_INPUT even if
1177:equal. Alias names must be unique and cannot collide with canonical args;
1178:deprecated/aliased combinations refuse. Canonical default/null validation
1179:semantics are unchanged. Generated TypeScript accepts either spelling and
1180:rejects both; capability comparison recognizes the old shape accepted by
1181:the alias. Compatibility advice for renamed same-typed inputs recommends
1182:mapping with an alias. It never recommends accepting and dropping the old
1183:value with `deprecated` as a rename repair.
1184:
1185:Every changed guide fence/starter runs as written against a freshly packed
1186:tarball. Qualification includes regression stores, actual old callers,
1187:reverse/native/preimage conflicts, forward old-source writes, inspection,
1188:hot/cold/check parity and copied wave-19 dormant lanes. Full test and
1189:differential results and any external qualification limits belong in
1190:WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. Reviews are mutually blind Muse and fresh Codex
1191:sessions on one immutable semantic revision, retained verbatim with
1192:provenance. A READY verdict records review readiness, not author acceptance
1193:or a publication receipt. No version bump or push is authorized.
1194:
1195:### 19.6 Review record
1196:
1197:Six mutually blind design rounds are preserved under `llp/reviews/3000.000.000-dorm-r*`. Round 6 received READY from Muse `muse-spark-1.3-contributor` (high, wrapper fallback) and a fresh Codex `gpt-6.1-sol` (high) on the same immutable snapshot; provenance includes its SHA-256 and both exact response digests. Earlier NOT READY findings drove disjoint writer admission, closed scalar/lifetime boundaries, per-cell restoration, explicit null/presence semantics and repeated-cycle recomputation. These are design readiness verdicts, not an implementation test result, author acceptance or a publication receipt. The commissioned implementation and qualification results are recorded in the workstream report.
1198:
1199:A final narrowly scoped confirmation (r7) also returned READY from both providers: protected rename kinds reject during compilation, preventing a forward-only protected transition. The unchanged full amendment remains covered by the r6 pair. Both exact outputs and delta provenance are preserved.

1201:## 20. Visible rollback views (ROLL)
1202:
1203:Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; commissioned for 0.4.39.
1204:This amendment replaces opt-in bridging for declared domain widenings and
1205:relaxed unique keys. Existing retention, metering, authority, immutable source,
1206:and atomic activation laws continue to apply. SEC7 owns ordinary check gates
1207:and ranking; ROLL owns deploy presentation and release selection.
1208:
1209:### 20.1 Prepared source and selection
1210:
1211:Deploy compares every selectable ordinary predecessor, even without sampled
1212:widened rows. A widening without an applicable authored down requires a compiled
1213:rollback view. All needed views must prepare successfully or deploy refuses.
1214:View admission compares structural source domains (including text minima and
1215:formats), nominal aliases, unique keys and caps; it never parses human advice
1216:strings to decide whether a view is needed. A fitting authored scalar down
1217:removes its covered value-domain risk; extraction/renames retain their typed
1218:identity laws.
1219:The compatible join is LLP 3000.000.000.002 §8: optional union, integer and text
1220:bound union with identical format, enum union, recursively joined bounded lists,
1221:and asset size-bound union with identical nominal kind, MIME and once policy.
1222:Nullability and list cardinality join separately. This differs from extraction,
1223:which requires full type equality, including size, optionality and cardinality.
1224:References join only across explicit nominal identity renames. A mixed change
1225:cannot enlarge any current shared-column domain with predecessor-only values:
1226:current read/write predicates may rely on their schema-level impossibility.
1227:The ordinary union view refuses even if that column is not the widening trigger.
1228:A closed alternative may retain the complete current authored source closure
1229:(schema, defaults, programs, helpers, jobs and authority), rather than synthesize
1230:a union. It requires every predecessor table and column under the same spelling,
1231:every predecessor operation name/kind, and current argument domains accepting
1232:every predecessor input under the same closed compatibility rule below. It
1233:retains the exact current domains and values, with no resurrection, translation
1234:or old-only writes. Missing fields/names or incompatible inputs refuse. Current
1235:result semantics are disclosed for old-UI review; current extra operations
1236:remain current-authorized. Native/worker bundle qualification remains mandatory.
1237:Otherwise author a compatible source and truthful migration before deploying. Enum order
1238:and nominal rename spelling do not change this domain comparison. Domain
1239:includes nullability, recursive list-element/cardinality bounds, text formats,
1240:integer bounds, asset kind/size/MIME/once constraints; unsupported or incompatible
1241:domain changes refuse rather than truncate or coerce. No conversion,
1242:zero-money fallback, implicit business default or arbitrary row choice is inferred.
1243:
1244:The view retains predecessor operation names and argument contracts, including
1245:existing authored operation aliases under their compilation law (same-module,
1246:no alias chains). Every exported predecessor alias counts as an old name; a
1247:current alias resolves to its actual authored target for input, guard, helper
1248:and authority comparison. Renaming an export never drops its current guards. A current
1249:require can be copied only with identical argument and entire transitive local
1250:binding/helper meanings, lookup predicates and control-flow paths. A changed
1251:unmodeled closure or guard depending on writes refuses. Canonical expression
1252:comparison ignores source locations, never semantics. When a relaxed unique key invalidates an old lookup, or current guards require
1253:an added field on a shared table, retain that field and its authored current
1254:backfill. An affected operation whose current guard/workflow cannot be transported
1255:unchanged may likewise reuse its current authored endpoint under the same closed
1256:input-compatibility rule. Every current added field is retained on a shared table, even without a guard
1257:mention; required fields retain their authored default/backfill and every old
1258:write path must supply them through a compatible current authored endpoint or
1259:refuse compilation. No missing-required-field write is permitted. All helpers in a reused workflow, including write,
1260:return and system-scope dependencies, need identical transitive meanings. An already-authored current endpoint of the same name/kind whose
1261:arguments accept every predecessor input may replace the affected old endpoint;
1262:the initial comparison permits identical argument types, optional widening and
1263:integer-bound widening and enum-superset widening, never narrowing or new required arguments. Retained
1264:current backfill identity keeps its output custody active. This supplies authored
1265:writes for the added required fields instead of inventing defaults; the generator never invents first-row
1266:selection. The replacement's point/limited selection must retain the language's
1267:deterministic total ordering, including its stable id tie-break; a source outside
1268:that deterministic language fragment refuses. Missing or incompatible authored
1269:endpoints refuse compilation.
1270:
1271:A generated visible view retains the current forward-migration declaration set
1272:for shared output columns. It does not resurrect a retired predecessor transform
1273:whose outputs already remain current columns in the compiled union. Historical
1274:source and receipts remain intact. This preserves current native values and
1275:migration custody instead of reinterpreting them with a retired input domain.
1276:Transforms needed for fields removed from current source remain subject to the
1277:ordinary truthful-projection and complete-adoption checks.
1278:
1279:A generated visible view omits a predecessor-only historical down declaration
1280:when all its outputs remain current columns whose generated domains admit the
1281:current native values. Its immutable original declaration and receipt evidence
1282:remain historical; the view does not reenter that retired projection or restore
1283:stale preimages over native values. Downs needed for removed fields remain.
1284:Exact original-source selection retains its projection/conflict behavior. Writer
1285:adoption still enforces full domains, lifetimes and zero-unacknowledged hiding.
1286:
1287:Shared tables retain current caps, immutability, final-existence constraints
1288:and lifecycle guards. Generated source carries authored current constraints,
1289:unique keys, expiry and opening declarations; the compiled view must preserve
1290:current hard constraints, reference-deletion/lifetime shape and sync audience
1291:or preparation refuses. Text constraints follow the explicit domain join, not
1292:a separate implicit relaxation. Schema quotas cannot become weaker merely
1293:because their current rule IR is unchanged. Unsupported structural transport
1294:requires an authored compatible workflow; no arbitrary population choice or
1295:new business default is generated.
1296:
1297:Shared tables retain the **current authored authority**, including transition
1298:bindings, read rules, payload guards and field ownership. The compiled bound IR
1299:must equal current authority after declared identity alignment. A release switch
1300:is a view over the same current authoritative rows, never restoration of a
1301:historical database snapshot: deleted rows stay deleted and current values
1302:stay current except declared truthful migrations. No resurrection or replay of
1303:historical preimages over authored current images is authorized. With no
1304:hiding and no domain enlarging beyond current, those row populations remain
1305:the current populations; changing read visibility still follows exact current
1306:authority.
1307:For intentional hiding, IR equality alone is insufficient: §20.2 additionally
1308:checks population-sensitive authority/guards under the actual writer. Removed old immutability/uniqueness
1309:constraints follow explicitly relaxed current constraints. Newer-only tables
1310:retain current rules without promising old clients expose new concepts. Native
1311:or worker dependencies without a qualified authored bundle refuse. Undefined
1312:fields, changed helper closures and incompatible workflows refuse rather than
1313:weakening guards. Nullable result fields and carried/reused guards appear in
1314:an action-focused checklist; successful mechanical adaptations are facts, not
1315:11–42 mandatory decisions. The commissioning author's chosen visible-rollback
1316:contract explicitly permits nullable/wider result domains; these are disclosed
1317:as RESULT compatibility changes, never claimed to retain a nonnull v1 result
1318:contract. Requiring nonnull results would contradict that requested end game.
1319:Names and accepted input contracts remain preserved. Dormant restoration advice
1320:names --restore-dormant.
1321:
1322:Removing a defaulted predecessor column requires an authored truthful down.
1323:The initial grammar has no acknowledged-absence escape for removed defaults.
1324:Preview may suggest a cross-table expression from declared references/columns,
1325:but it is advisory source for the author to review, never an inferred business
1326:mapping. A false default is not a reversible representation.
1327:
1328:Prepared source is immutable. Each parent manifest maps original target id to
1329:numeric view id, executable digest, changed columns, carried guards and hiding
1330:reason. The parent's full control digest includes the manifest; the view's full
1331:control digest includes its metadata. Prepared views are usable only after the
1332:parent's CAS activation commits and never count as ordinary latest or equal
1333:forward source. Failed parents remove stages/views. Unknown admitted outcomes
1334:retain them for history-authoritative recovery; uncommitted parents cannot
1335:authorize selection. Before either equal-source no-op or retained-source reactivation, prepare the
1336:complete desired view set privately and compare target/executable digest/hiding
1337:reasons with that source's own committed manifest, verifying its entries. A
1338:matching set permits no-op or identity reuse. A missing, stale or newly needed
1339:mapping creates an ordinary registration parent using the same source and full
1340:adoption/CAS validation; no view is selectable before that commit. Output and
1341:JSON identify the equivalent retained source as sourceRelease. Source and old
1342:manifests remain immutable. Temporary desired views are removed on no-op/reuse,
1343:refusal and preview. This also upgrades unchanged legacy deployments without
1344:needed registrations. Later equal source is a no-op once its desired set matches.
1345:Retained-source reactivation and rollback preserve their own registry context.
1346:
1347:Resolve the manifest belonging to the active ordinary release, or the
1348:registered parent of the active compiled view, under the command lock. Never
1349:borrow a later deployment's view across source branches: it may carry weaker
1350:guards than the active source. If that context has no mapping, default selection
1351:uses original source with the same writer visibility checks. Bind actual source
1352:digest, expected release and activation revision to the existing CAS. ABA/equal-content switches change revision. Cold, hot,
1353:preview and restart use that actual immutable source. Selection restores dormant
1354:names implicitly, with full data/constraint/lifetime validation. Output and
1355:releases identify original and actual source. --exact selects the original and
1356:reports dormant hiding; it bypasses only default visible-view selection, never
1357:applicable security/strict refusals, authority checks or migration validation.
1358:
1359:### 20.2 Writer-enforced visibility
1360:
1361:Default rollback, even without a registered view, admits zero unacknowledged
1362:hidden predecessor-table rows. Candidate preview also refuses uncovered hiding
1363:on reverse snapshots. Future bad projections refuse selection under the writer.
1364:Preview populations are privileged retained-row counts, not viewer guarantees.
1365:
1366:The initial intentional-hiding spelling is:
1367:`rollback N hides TABLE where COLUMN = null because 'REASON'`.
1368:The candidate column must be nullable, the predecessor column nonnullable, and
1369:table/column spellings identical. N is the immutable original. No arbitrary
1370:predicate, blanket exemption or rename combination is admitted. Its view keeps
1371:the null restriction while joining the nonnull domain: nullable 0..1000 versus
1372:required 0..100 becomes required 0..1000, preserving widened nonnull values.
1373:The nonempty reason travels in immutable metadata and repeats on selection.
1374:
1375:Under the actual writer transaction, capture the before image, run full adoption,
1376:then compare every retained hidden predecessor row. Only acknowledged null rows
1377:and the actual hidden-reference closure rooted in them are allowed; unrelated
1378:hidden rows refuse atomically. The closure graph contains only scalar/optional
1379:nominal Ref cells in before-image active rows, as typed by the source schema;
1380:ids are aligned through the catalog identity. An edge qualifies only when its
1381:parent was also active before, is actually retained-hidden after adoption, and
1382:has no invalid reference-lifetime marker. No list, synthetic extraction anchor,
1383:asset, dormant overlay, cascade or set-null declaration invents an edge. A
1384:renamed identity uses its same catalog number. Before-hidden rows are outside
1385:this newly-hidden population; newer-only tables are distinguished. The snapshot
1386:preview is advisory; concurrent writes cannot bypass activation revalidation.
1387:Acknowledgment never changes physical preservation or permits broader read/write
1388:authority. After collecting the actual newly hidden table set, the writer rejects
1389:negative existence authority, reference comparisons that can admit absence, or
1390:population-tainted requirements/control flow. Positive existence requirements
1391:may only become denials. A direct point assertion may discharge absence taint:
1392:if its row is hidden it aborts before subsequent work; its surviving field values
1393:remain unchanged. Writes to affected existing targets require that same exact
1394:point-presence guard. Transitive bindings and compiler-expanded helper bodies
1395:are followed; unknown paths refuse. Tainted branch/loop/conditional-write keys AND persisted write values,
1396:negative/count-based guard dependencies, nested job-query scope and maintained
1397:populations depending on hidden sources are conservatively outside this initial
1398:acknowledgment form. This is a closed syntactic admission check, not an inferred
1399:semantic implication or a finite-snapshot security proof. Explicit reasons do
1400:not bypass it. Source may retain the safety witness in a visible bridge instead.
1401:Ordinary hidden-row occupancy/retention laws remain explicit in the preview;
1402:intentional hiding supplies no promise of automatic forward conflict resolution.
1403:
1404:Point-presence proofs discharge taint only for subsequent work in program
1405:order on that exact table/key. A guard cannot justify any earlier tainted read
1406:or write. Mutations/jobs retain their existing atomic transaction law: a later
1407:refusal rolls back all prior writes in the operation, including work before a
1408:point assertion. The population checker does not weaken that transaction law.
1409:
1410:### 20.3 Edited transformation outputs
1411:
1412:An edited in-place migration output reverses through an applicable down on its
1413:assembled current image, or stays exact if the compiled selected view's full
1414:type/constraints accept that physical value. This never widens a schema per row,
1415:coerces, truncates, null-fills or invents defaults. Otherwise selection refuses.
1416:Original preimage, actual authored newer output, prior computation and physical
1417:old-view presence are separate evidence; edited output never gains native birth.
1418:
1419:Forward validates old-view field presence/null/default/lifetime evidence first.
1420:Preserving authored output requires unchanged explicit arguments AND freshly
1421:unchanged computation, including point/existence lookups. Changed inputs require
1422:exact deterministic agreement with authored output or a real conflict printing
1423:competing values. Unedited outputs retain preimage precedence; receipts state
1424:when older views read stale until forward. Repeated and partially retired cycles,
1425:invalid lifetimes, pruned evidence and meter cuts obey the same atomic laws.
1426:
1427:### 20.4 Reference renames and asset extraction
1428:
1429:Reference renamed-from transports the existing physical cell and reference
1430:lifetime bindings, including retained/narrowed images. It never recaptures a
1431:deleted/reused parent or captured/engine-owned field. Both aliases at once and
1432:unequal destination cells refuse. After identity alignment, nominal target,
1433:nullability, bounds, on-delete and capture shape must be exactly equal; widening
1434:is a separate migration, not part of this rename.
1435:
1436:`extract CHILD from PARENT via REFERENCE: {newField: oldField}` is a declarative
1437:migration, not an operation or cross-table rename. It creates one receipt-owned
1438:initial child per source row; child's deterministic id equals parent id in the
1439:child namespace. The child has a required reference to parent with cascade or
1440:restrict deletion; keep/set-null are outside the initial form. Independent active
1441:or dormant destination history refuses. At least one mapped asset is required,
1442:with identical full source/child type. Parent must remove that source asset
1443:column. Other copied fields must be explicitly immutable scalar source fields;
1444:mutable, captured, maintained and nominal-reference copies refuse. Copies must
1445:continue to agree both directions. Other required child columns need authored
1446:defaults. Receipt identity uses numeric parent/child catalog identities and declared column
1447:origins, so table, asset-column and uncaptured reference-column aliases do not
1448:create a new migration or recapture an anchor. Typed schema normalization updates
1449:extraction table/reference slots and preserves source/destination column strings.
1450:Destination admission inspects active rows, dormant rows/cells and competing
1451:receipts. Reentry requires its existing receipt-owned child and valid anchor;
1452:pruned/deleted child history is never recreated from metadata.
1453:
1454:Future independent children with distinct ids are allowed; extraction is not
1455:unique-parent. Existing durable id reservation and duplicate-key admission apply
1456:to receipt-owned/dormant children exactly as to ordinary rows. The normal mint
1457:never overwrites an occupied id; an explicitly forced collision refuses. Neither
1458:rollback nor deleting receipt metadata frees a still-retained row id.
1459:
1460:Asset ledger home and physical asset cell move together, without reuploading or
1461:changing bytes. The former active cell and equal dormant overlay are removed;
1462:a distinct overlay refuses rather than being discarded. Private displaced-cell
1463:receipt evidence persists separately from active cells. Reverse removes the
1464:child asset cell while retaining identity/scalars and newer-only children. Later
1465:forward uses the current old-view asset, including replacement/optional clearing,
1466:never reviving released receipt bytes or creating a second ledger home.
1467:
1468:A native child without a receipt reverses only when exactly one total candidate child
1469:exists for that parent, and that child has a valid private parent-lifetime anchor. Anchors are captured while
1470:extraction is active, including absent-parent invalid anchors; unchanged writes
1471:never recapture invalid evidence. Older independent relations cannot qualify
1472:retroactively. Deletion/id reuse, reparenting and ambiguity refuse; no arbitrary
1473:photo selection. Receipt-owned children likewise require valid lifetime evidence.
1474:
1475:For row-dependent audiences the child must read through its parent with identical
1476:parent policy; otherwise source/child read policies must be identical and row-
1477:independent, or destination denies reads. Existing payload gates must match.
1478:Reverse must preserve the current effective child/parent/column audience or
1479:refuse with guarded-bridge advice. These are closed admission rules, not a
1480:policy implication prover. Native/ref compatibility does not confer byte access.
1481:
1482:Actual activation revalidates active AND dormant destination emptiness, regular
1483:byte presence/ledger length, full types, scalar copies, parent binding, anchor
1484:validity and reverse ambiguity inside adoption under command locking and CAS.
1485:Preview runs this transaction on a private copy; its counts cannot authorize a
1486:later activation. Failure rolls back evidence, format, homes, selection and
1487:generation together. Devices execute no migration metadata or home authority:
1488:authenticated serving resolves the current server ledger home and current row/
1489:column policy. Existing device generation and media-retention receipts remain;
1490:this introduces no retroactive cache-erasure claim.
1491:
1492:Store format 9 fences edited-output/reference/extraction AND compiled-view
1493:registry custody from older runtimes. Every successful release adoption upgrades
1494:to 9 transactionally, before prepared registry entries become selectable. Older
1495:CLIs must not mistake view ids for ordinary latest or skip visibility policy.
1496:Every hot activation request from this CLI carries the explicit registry-custody
1497:feature field, including exact/original-source selection, an empty registry and
1498:an existing format-8 store. The field covers all format-9 adoption laws, including
1499:edited outputs; it is not conditional on a visible view. Updated hosts reject
1500:activation clients omitting it. Older hosts reject that unknown field before
1501:admission; restart serve with the updated runtime. Updated hosts always require
1502:it before activation admission, with no conditional old-wire-shape exception.
1503:This prevents an older engine from executing any adoption without the new laws.
1504:Unchanged no-op deployments without new custody can stay at 8. Format 8 opens;
1505:failed adoption leaves its old format unchanged; 9 survives
1506:old-source selection. Capabilities release-child-extraction-v1 and
1507:release-reference-renames-v1 fence schema admission. Device projections omit
1508:migration metadata while retaining ordinary current tables and through policies.
1509:
1510:### 20.5 Presentation and qualification
1511:
1512:Human deploy output starts with verdict/security FIX/test status, followed by
1513:adoption/rollback facts. Result advice groups a changed source column with its
1514:affected operations once. Security review is last, scoped to changed declarations,
1515:ranked and capped at five advisory notes, with omitted count. Reachable FIX
1516:witnesses are never capped/suppressed. A trailing verdict supports tail readers.
1517:JSON carries every compatibility detail, report, test status and view mapping.
1518:Campaign matching/stale/missing/incomplete state and age remain explicit. Finite
1519:tests never certify universal security. SEC7 owns ordinary check ranking/gates.
1520:
1521:Readiness requires blind Muse and fresh Codex READY reviews of the same revision,
1522:retained verbatim with provenance. Qualification covers cycles/conflicts, optional
1523:money, image/file homes, deleted/reused references, view failure/CAS/restart,
1524:bakeoff copies and packed guide cases. This record claims no unrun test or
1525:publication and authorizes no push/version bump.

259:## 11. Old-client operation contracts (REL5)
260:
261:Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-19; target 0.4.34. This
262:amends §3: new required parameters remain required in generated types, including
263:with a compatibility baseline. Added result fields retain release-spanning
264:fallbacks. check/dev with client-compatibility.json report old-client hazards
265:against that saved source; deploy/check and rollback/check compare the active
266:release's operation contracts to the candidate. Report removed or changed-kind
267:operations, added required arguments, removed arguments (unknown inputs refuse),
268:retyped/narrowed arguments, changed result shapes/domains and changed authored
269:refusals. A conservative operation footprint retains consumed source column domains
270:(including projected int/text bounds across writes, loops, merges and aggregates).
271:Write payload columns and consulted index-key domains participate even when
272:the operation returns a scalar, since validation can change its refusal behavior.
273:Guard/order dependencies and ambiguous same-name source fields may also appear
274:in RESULT REVIEW; this is an advisory to verify results/refusals, not a claim
275:that a returned value necessarily differs. A column never consumed by the
276:operation does not change this evidence. Declared action result domains compare
277:their full wire contracts, independently of TypeScript number/string erasure.
278:Each report names operation and source release/baseline, distinguishes
279:input breaks from result/behavior review, and gives an actionable repair. Correct
280:authorization tightening remains intentional behavior to verify, not an input
281:break. Static comparison is conservative and does not certify arbitrary behavior.
282:
283:Quarry query/mutation parameters admit `name: T default <literal>`: omission
284:or null uses the fitting non-null literal, explicit non-null values validate
285:against T. Only bounded scalar literals (including negative integers) are
286:admitted; now/viewer/expressions and null defaults refuse. The compiler lowers
287:to an optional wire argument and ordinary coalescing setup in the shared IR;
288:the body sees T, generated callers may omit it. This adds no wire vocabulary.
289:`name: T deprecated` accepts a validated optional input but does not bind it
290:in the operation body; using it refuses compilation. This lets old callers send
291:an obsolete field while new callers omit it. It does not silently accept unknown
292:inputs. Modifiers on helper/shape/action/job/native declarations refuse explicitly
293:until their distinct invocation contracts support them.
294:
295:`alias oldName = currentName` declares a query/mutation alias in the same
296:module. It clones the complete operation declaration before ordinary binding,
297:so validation, rules, guards, results, prediction classification and metering
298:remain the target's. Aliases target authored non-alias operations, cannot chain
299:or cycle, and collisions/unknown targets refuse. No alias can bypass authority.
300:Keep the alias until the old client population has retired.
301:
302:Availability accepts extra optional served parameters; existing generated
303:parameter types must still match and no extra served required parameter may
304:exist. SDK and self-contained generated predicates share this contract. It
305:remains capability evidence, never authorization or result-semantic proof.
306:
307:Acceptance covers omitted/null/explicit/invalid defaults through server and
308:device, ignored deprecated arguments and forbidden references, aliases including
309:module collisions and refusal parity, generated requiredness and availability
310:parity, argument/result/refusal diff controls, baseline warnings, and a packed
311:`guide deploy` v2 recipe whose frozen v1 calls still run with no client edits.

449:## 11. Wave-22 loop repairs (DX6)
450:
451:Commission: Charlie Cheever's wave-22 fixes #5, #6 and #8 (2026-10-22).
452:Implementer: Codex in sb4-b22-dx6. This amends §10, the staging clock of
453:LLP 3000.000.000.002 §4, supplied sessions in LLP 3000.036.004 §15.4,
454:and the starter application of LLP 3000.013.002 §2. SEC7 owns security advice,
455:intent, campaigns, upload recipes and the password/roles recipe.
456:This proposal is subject to the requested fresh Muse and Codex reviews.
457:
458:### Session input and compatibility
459:
460:Seeded records preserve the actual signup session's kind alongside its
461:principal, token and numeric expiresAt. They can be passed directly as Session.
462:Online createClient accepts a legacy incomplete session literal as token-only
463:transport credentials: its token must be nonempty; partial principal, kind and
464:expiry metadata never establishes identity, refresh timing or authority. The
465:server resolves the bearer; session() is null and there is no refresh timer,
466:as for the existing token option. Complete shapes remain strictly validated
467:and privately snapshotted. Local/device session input remains complete-only.
468:E_SESSION lists every missing/invalid field without values and gives the exact
469:one-line online repair createClient({ url, token }). An ISO expiry in a complete
470:shape refuses instead of creating a NaN timer. Restored full sessions retain
471:the existing restore validation and server expiry semantics.
472:
473:### Advances, maintenance and retained boundaries
474:
475:ClockAdvance remains an object reporting now/fired/held; valueOf returns now
476:for arithmetic, ordering and new Date(result). This is JavaScript numeric
477:coercion, not strict-equality compatibility; typed callers use result.now.
478:
479:For classified staging, advance(duration) preserves remaining ordinary session
480:lifetimes by default. The core advances valid full/read session created/expiry
481:fields, read caps and recoverable mint receipts by the same checked delta,
482:in the same transaction as existing worker-lease preservation, before publishing
483:the new process clock. Expired, capped, revoked or superseded authority cannot
484:become live. Selection uses the authority kernel and bounded pages; overflow
485:refuses atomically. Existing action delegated sessions and supervised service
486:families remain owned by the worker-lease path, without double shifting.
487:Historical admission timestamps are evidence, not an ongoing cap; application
488:deadlines/backoffs remain business time.
489:
490:Preservation includes SDK custody, not just raw HTTP credentials. A staging
491:response for the exact request bearer can publish authoritative session expiry
492:and read cap beside the same locked business-time/revision snapshot, obtained
493:through a detached store-identity/liveness-checked authority read. No bearer,
494:new authority, mint, rotation or refresh is exposed. Production publishes no
495:such metadata. A failed detached timing read suppresses the clock sample,
496:including streaming-final samples, instead of publishing advanced business
497:time with stale credential metadata. A client reconciles only its current
498:bearer's matching request URL/header and monotonically increases its live
499:expiry/cap before clock listeners run. Local/device clients await durable
500:credential custody before publishing or persisting their business clock.
501:Online clients keep a volatile clock; their existing session listeners may
502:persist custody asynchronously. Authenticated restoration still validates
503:expired metadata with the staging probe; no online durability barrier is implied.
504:Heartbeat final frames carry the same matching-bearer timing or unavailable
505:evidence as ordinary response headers. Reconciliation and the corresponding
506:local/device clock publication are serialized: durable credential saves are awaited
507:before publishing or persisting the advanced clock. A failed save leaves the
508:clock unchanged, retains dirty credential timing for retry, and surfaces as a
509:transport failure; rotation/close fences also suppress stale clock publication.
510:App preparation retains its existing private credential staging: prepared local
511:clock offsets remain non-durable, including on discard/close, until the actual
512:App credential commit succeeds. Only then may clock persistence activate before
513:the scope is published. A later clock-save failure reports a signed-in warning
514:and retries through ordinary device work; it cannot roll back durable identity.
515:The existing per-invocation response cutoff (LLP 3000.013 §6 and §12.7) also
516:spans implicit scope retirement's local credential cleanup. A scope-owned
517:reference count retains capture before teardown and through each scheduled
518:local credential task, releasing on rollback or close without waiting for an
519:auth operation or remote revocation queue.
520:Capture occurs synchronously when the response decoder validates a body,
521:before caller Promise continuations can cross that cutoff.
522:An invocation retains a body validated while live through settlement; a
523:completed capture owner cannot reopen its cutoff through another begin or hold.
524:Retention preserves a decoded body, not a stale authentication classification.
525:An E_AUTH refusal remains scoped to the bearer revision actually dispatched:
526:rotation supersedes prior and intermediate retiring-bearer revisions, including
527:when the response was decoded before rotation or arrives after cancellation or
528:a retryable refresh failure. Recheck that authority when consuming retained
529:responses. A successor already dispatched during credential persistence remains
530:current; its own refusal may sign it out. Sign-out alone does not supersede a
531:same-bearer refusal, and sent outcomes and business refusals retain their ordinary
532:invocation custody. Retryable refresh failure still permits a same-id retry under
533:the current credential revision.
534:Rotated/logged-out credentials cannot be restored by a late response. Client
535:clocks are isolated, so one bearer's response cannot expire another before its
536:own timing is reconciled. Read startup verifies before scheduling expiry;
537:an expired restored metadata image requires a successful authenticated read-only
538:staging probe with matching viewer/scope and live timing. Production/offline
539:without this evidence keeps the existing expiry refusal. The local constructor
540:reconciles bootstrap timing before applying a restored device clock's expiry
541:check. Tests retain actual read clients and restore them across their old
542:absolute expiry, compare two independent bearers, and exercise explicit-expiry
543:and production controls. These headers are staging timing evidence only;
544:ordinary authority is still checked on every server admission. Real elapsed time still consumes
545:credentials. sessions:'expire' explicitly tests expiry with the existing
546:business clock. Dev retains its existing expiry behavior. Production rejects
547:all clock controls and never calls preservation. Restart keeps the persisted credential timestamps. Staging clock persistence
548:now follows §12; ordinary dev keeps its process-local offset.
549:This intentional staging-only simulated jump also shifts a read family's cap;
550:ordinary production read-cap immutability is unchanged.
551:
552:advance(duration, {drain:false}) advances time and pauses background maintenance
553:before releasing the writer. This covers expiry sweeps, scheduled enqueue,
554:queued job chunks, asset collection, view recount and delivery maintenance,
555:so a mutation can exercise its own deadline check. Authorization and logical
556:row expiry still apply to ordinary queries/mutations. The pause lasts until
557:host.drain(), another draining advance, or process restart. drain() uses the
558:same authenticated endpoint with duration:'0ms', drain:true, advancing no time
559:and resuming normal coalesced maintenance. eachBoundary requires drain:true.
560:The HTTP report includes maintenancePaused; native CLI spells these controls
561:clock advance D --no-drain and clock drain. Failed/ambiguous requests are never
562:automatically replayed. A failed drain leaves its advanced time intact, its
563:report explicitly incomplete, and maintenance paused. Only a successful explicit
564:drain resumes it. drain() advances no time and safely continues existing queued
565:work/checkpoints; never repeat a lost advance to obtain that recovery.
566:
567:A saved boundary is future-held only when it is strictly greater than the
568:boundary of the requested effective schedule window. Equality means that the
569:current window was already processed: report not-due, without consulting wall
570:time. Repeated advances inside that window and zero-time drains succeed without
571:allowHeld, including recovery of queued work after a failed drain completion.
572:Never enqueue the processed window again.
573:A draining advance whose saved future boundary blocks requested work refuses
574:E_CLOCK_HELD before clock, session or lease effects. The refusal names the
575:schedule, saved boundary, next unprocessed window and recovery duration to
576:reach the saved boundary from the unchanged process clock, not from the rejected
577:requested time. Relative recovery hints in accepted reports likewise use the
578:actual process clock, including when a disclosure floor is ahead of it.
579:A held report sets heldUntil to that saved boundary and
580:nextBoundary to the next unprocessed boundary. Catching up to heldUntil
581:reports not-due and never re-enqueues the processed window; reaching nextBoundary
582:fires once. Fresh staging storage and continuing with the original offset are
583:recovery choices; saved markers never rewind. allowHeld:true (CLI --allow-held) explicitly accepts a
584:retained boundary. Intentional drain:false pauses and dev --hold-schedules are
585:explicit holds. not-due/initialization remains a successful reported outcome.
586:Control authorization/classification is checked again under the actual writer.
587:
588:### Upgrade advice and future formats
589:
590:Advice is scoped to actual version transitions and each behavior's introduction
591:version. A fresh init/check is silent. Delivered stderr is acknowledged durably
592:for non-TTY callers too; baseline checks neither emit nor acknowledge advice.
593:The new upgrade helper retains warning stderr even under --quiet. Published
594:older quiet CLIs discard their check child's stderr without signalling this;
595:that historical capture cannot be distinguished from normal non-TTY delivery.
596:Advice from that old quiet path may therefore be acknowledged without display;
597:guide changes remains available. No ancestor
598:process inspection or script mutation is introduced.
599:
600:The bounded candidate scan covers partial session literals and numeric advance
601:uses, explicit persistent startDev calls and data --json consumers. Explicit
602:nearby refusal assertions do not become dev-door warnings. Candidate paths,
603:never source or credentials, are printed; incomplete scans remain disclosed.
604:Future known-family store formats refuse with 'a newer Snapback (FORMAT)'.
605:Read-only SQL inspection validates the format before exposing rows and exits
606:nonzero on refusal. Already published old binaries cannot be repaired.
607:
608:### Starter and proof rendering
609:
610:Both list starters render through the packaged ReadState. Proven absence and
611:partial empty results retain distinct text, with existing freshness and
612:pagination semantics unchanged. Rename the docs' local ReadState example to
613:ReadNotice so it does not shadow the export. Password clients omit persona
614:selection and explicit web witness/generated API imports. The proof driver installs the
615:existing witness bridge before app creation; createApp calls an immutable Symbol observer only under explicit witness=web.
616:This is a public same-origin test channel, not a secret or an authentication
617:boundary. A witness URL alone installs no observer and exposes no App global.
618:The driver installs its nonconfigurable/nonwritable observer before application
619:scripts and locks/freezes the selected witness bridge; the first App remains
620:selected for that page. Same-App explicit API registration can enrich it;
621:a different explicit App refuses E_WITNESS_APP_SELECTION and requires a separate
622:page. Later automatic Apps cannot silently redirect the proof. Application JS
623:remains trusted to implement the application: same-origin code can forge its
624:own test observer outside a driven page, just as it can read web session storage.
625:The observer grants no new credentials or backend authority. Ordinary bundles
626:carry only the creation dispatch, not the witness implementation. No-API
627:registration captures only durable non-ephemeral mutation handles; direct
628:witness mutate still requires generated API.
629:
630:Proof text locators match normalized entire descendant text, including composed
631:React Native spans and wrapping labels. Choose smallest matching visible,
632:non-editable elements; independent matches retain strict Playwright ambiguity.
633:Whitespace normalization collapses runs and trims both ends; case is preserved,
634:with no Unicode normalization or input value matching. Exact strings are case-sensitive;
635:explicit substring strings are case-insensitive and regex follows its pattern/flags.
636:String matching stays exact by default; substring is explicit, never a silent
637:fallback. A composed label cannot make an unrelated sibling count as success.
638:Text ranges outside overflow clips do not contribute evidence to a visible
639:ancestor. Layoutless wrappers may forward their visible descendants; a clipped
640:suffix cannot manufacture a complete status label.

689:## 12. Wave-24 loop repairs (DX7)
690:
691:Commission: Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; implementer: Codex in sb4-b24-dx7.
692:This amends §11, LLP 3000.036 §22.8 and the ordinary rule-helper exclusion.
693:SEC8 owns security questionnaire/dispositions, presence intent, invariant test
694:coverage and lifecycle advice; DX7 only reports their existing verdicts.
695:Implementation and tests accompany this amendment; review readiness is distinct
696:from author acceptance.
697:
698:### Durable staging time
699:
700:A staging store owns its cumulative offset and latest explicitly checkpointed
701:business time. The additive typed metadata record also retains a pending
702:maintenance pause. Production ignores this record and rejects clock controls;
703:ordinary dev retains its process-local clock/reset contract. No release-format
704:change or filesystem sidecar is needed. Store adapters use their existing
705:metadata transaction and fencing, including Postgres.
706:
707:Startup resumes at the maximum of wall plus saved offset, checkpointed time,
708:and the persisted time_audience_clock disclosure floor (LLP 3000.000.000.002
709:§4), plus the maximum retained commit stamp and schedule boundary even when a
710:checkpoint exists. A fresh store with no evidence starts at wall time. Unknown
711:metadata is preserved by ordinary old-binary adoptions; if an older binary or
712:manual restore removes it, recover time from these retained floors, with no
713:claim to recover a deleted pause. Explicit startup
714:offsets (serve --clock-offset and startServe({clockOffset})) are absolute
715:offsets from wall time, clamped to the resumed floor;
716:restarting never adds the same offset twice or rewinds saved boundaries.
717:The resulting effective offset is persisted at startup, preserving the saved
718:paused flag exactly. This is the maximum of five named evidence values: wall
719:plus offset, checkpointed now, time_audience_clock, recent commit maximum and
720:latest schedule boundary (plus an explicitly requested offset floor). Removing
721:all time evidence by restoring/recreating a store can return it to wall time;
722:this is the intentional independent-test reset path, never an in-place rewind.
723:Old binaries do not enforce the pause even when they preserve its record;
724:downgrade is not a way to run a paused proof under these guarantees. Malformed checkpoints
725:refuse startup rather than silently resetting. Downgrades to older binaries
726:ignore this additive record and retain their historical process-local behavior.
727:
728:Each accepted staging advance checkpoints offset, time and paused=true in the
729:same transaction that preserves eligible sessions and worker leases, before
730:publishing the process clock or doing maintenance. E_CLOCK_HELD and input validation precede this transaction. A definite
731:storage abort rolls back all those effects. Postgres executes the existing
732:fenced writer transaction containing offset/time/pause and session/lease edits.
733:An uncertain COMMIT may already have committed: E_COMMIT_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN fences
734:the host from further admission/maintenance, then shuts it down. Restart reads
735:the durable checkpoint before serving; never replay a credential shift to
736:reconcile it. An uncertain pause-clear may already have cleared it, so only
737:definite failure guarantees retention of the durable pause. Fault injection
738:covers both write boundaries. A lost reply is still ambiguous and never automatically
739:replayed. drain:false, a failed drain and an interrupted drain retain the pause
740:across restart. A successful drain durably clears it only after due work has
741:finished. If that final write fails, report an incomplete drain and retain the
742:pause. Startup writes a checkpoint with zero delta and never preserves credentials.
743:Each advance computes its delta from the resumed effective clock and writes
744:credential timestamps and checkpoint in one commit: the checkpoint itself is
745:the durable completion evidence; there is no separate applied marker. A restart
746:does not shift already-preserved credentials again. Clock
747:status/advance reports say the offset belongs to this staging store. Coalescing,
748:eachBoundary, current-window equality and explicit allowHeld keep §11 semantics.
749:
750:### Rendered and scrollable text
751:
752:Mounted text inside overflow:auto/scroll ancestors counts even when outside
753:the scrollport: it can be reached by scrolling. The selector does not scroll
754:or change application layout. Overflow:hidden/clip remains a real clip, with
755:per-axis character geometry retaining only reachable characters. Starting at
756:each text rectangle, compose legal scroll shifts [current-max,current-min]
757:and each ancestor clip from inner to outer, independently for x and y; this
758:excludes unreachable negative-origin text and admits an inner scrollport
759:inside an outer hidden clip without scrolling the page. Zero-sized scrollports
760:remain clips. visibility:visible descendants may override visibility:hidden;
761:display:none and opacity:0 suppress every descendant. content-visibility:auto
762:counts when browser range geometry exists (unrendered skipped content supplies
763:no geometry); content-visibility:hidden excludes. Use computed CSS overflow
764:values, including the browser's visible-to-auto cross-axis conversion. Fixed
765:and sticky descendants use current geometry only, without hypothetical ancestor
766:scroll movement; the clip model is conservative for their special containing
767:blocks and does not claim they can be revealed by scrolling. display:none,
768:visibility:hidden/collapse, opacity:0 and content-visibility:hidden remain
769:excluded, as do editable/input/script text and layoutless text. This does not
770:claim occlusion detection or virtualized content that is not mounted. Existing
771:normalization, smallest matching element and strict ambiguity remain unchanged.
772:
773:### Upgrade and gate presentation
774:
775:Upgrade snapshots count security notes alongside compiler notes and report
776:pending review gates separately, with unavailable previous gate data stated
777:honestly for old snapshots. The gate status reflects acknowledgments without
778:writing them during reporting. A refuted invariant cannot be acknowledged:
779:check explicitly reports that --ack-security was not applied, leaves review
780:records untouched and explains that the policy/intent must be repaired.
781:The JSON field acknowledgmentNotApplied carries the same explanation. The
782:existing Report::refuted predicate determines refutations (LLP 3000.036.004);
783:ordinary advisory notes never fail check. Only check acknowledgment paths
784:write dispositions, and delta reporting only reads them. The CLI takes one
785:--ack-security ID per invocation; when refuted, that entire single-ID write is
786:suppressed and check exits nonzero. Old upgrade CLIs invoke the newly installed
787:check and cannot acknowledge/bypass its gates. The advance scan is bounded
788:heuristic migration advice, never a security gate; indirect aliases or arbitrary
789:use(t) dataflow can be missed. Mixed failures show
790:schema/device remediation before the gate repair/ack command, explicitly
791:subject to repairing any refutation first. Gate-only upgrade failures name
792:check --ack-security <ID> --reason '<spec rationale>'; other failures retain
793:schema/device remediation. An already published old CLI may still print its
794:old E_UPGRADE line; the new check child must carry the actionable explanation.
795:The release card records the 0.4.37 CLI's obsolete closed-door/startDev advice.
796:The numeric-advance candidate scan requires an actual advance call in the same
797:local expression/assignment as numeric consumption; unrelated operators or
798:new Date/assert calls elsewhere in a file do not qualify.
799:
800:### Protected immutable payloads
801:
802:A column-read-protected payload may be immutable. On insert its supplied value
803:is validated normally. On every update/existing-upsert, a supplied protected
804:immutable field is ignored without comparing it with stored data, for every
805:viewer, including its readers and the system. Its retained value and asset
806:placement remain unchanged; no upload ownership or placement check runs for
807:the ignored field because no asset is adopted (amending LLP 3000.036 §22.9).
808:An ignored newly uploaded asset remains unplaced under the existing uploader
809:ownership, accounting and collection lifetime; no extra adoption or extension
810:is created. Immutability constrains update/existing-upsert within one row
811:lifetime; delete is independently authorized by the table delete rule. It
812:does not add an undeletable identity policy or prohibit a newly authorized
813:insert after deletion. The operation still validates supplied input types/bounds; ignoring occurs
814:before merge/placement, including explicit null for an optional field. Invalid
815:operation input still fails using schema/site facts alone. An
816:existing upsert differing only in these fields succeeds with them retained;
817:absent-upsert still inserts. Compiler advice on an
818:explicit patch names that behavior using schema/site facts only. Device
819:prediction retains the old cell or its omission and never predicts the supplied
820:cell. Ordinary jobs/system updates follow the same rule; schema-evolution
821:backfills remain the explicit replacement path. Ordinary unprotected immutability retains
822:its existing contract. Cap/unique/index/authority/derived dependencies on
823:protected fields remain refused. This input-independent policy preserves
824:LLP 3000.036 §22.8's noninterference oracle and needs no new device protocol:
825:the same interpreter runs it on the server and device.
826:
827:### Audience helpers and hidden probes
828:
829:Expression-bodied helpers, or a block containing one return, may appear in
830:table/column rules when expansion is exactly the existing rule grammar.
831:Arguments are ordinary typed rule terms, substituted simultaneously without
832:caller capture. Only parameters, viewer and rule constants may be free in the
833:helper; a helper's focal dot cannot capture the caller's row. Nested helpers
834:are bounded to eight levels and 4096 visited syntax nodes per rule (including
835:arguments and each expanded body), refused as E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR on
836:exhaustion. Direct and indirect cycles are detected on the expansion stack.
837:Parameters cannot shadow viewer or rule constants. exists is explicitly
838:allowed and pays the same probes after expansion/inversion as handwritten
839:rules; ordinary reads are excluded. Simultaneous substitution prevents capture;
840:cycles,
841:wrong arity/types, block effects/lets, arithmetic, clock and ordinary reads
842:refuse with E_QUARRY_RULE_OPERATOR and an actionable repair. No new runtime
843:instruction, probe semantics, authority or resource allowance is introduced.
844:Expansion precedes read-through, index inference, audience inversion and
845:security analysis so every consumer sees the same canonical predicate.
846:
847:Program exists remains a caller-visible read; rule exists retains its existing
848:policy probe semantics. N_UNREADABLE_PROBE and N_LOOKUP_FILTERED name the exact
849:hiding read/read-through rule and source site, including exists directly in a
850:require. No runtime refusal confirms whether a hidden row physically exists.
851:Runnable guide recipes demonstrate protected immutable payloads, a role helper
852:and a hidden parent probe alongside a deliberately minimal readable projection.
853:

855:
856:Regression coverage includes repeated startServe/advance/drain runs on one
857:store, drain:false restart and legacy saved boundaries; no double session
858:shift; production controls and failed-checkpoint atomicity; mounted below-fold
859:RN-web text and hidden/clip controls; gate-only and refuted upgrade diagnostics;

865:qualify-linux --consumer-only (test.sh is prohibited), published 0.4.35/0.4.37
866:old-CLI upgrades, refs/docs budget and repository-root checks are recorded in
867:WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md. No version bump, publication or push.
