## 20. Visible rollback views (ROLL)

Implementer: Codex for Charlie Cheever, 2026-10-22; commissioned for 0.4.39.
This amendment replaces opt-in bridging for declared domain widenings and
relaxed unique keys. Existing retention, metering, authority, immutable source,
and atomic activation laws continue to apply. SEC7 owns ordinary check gates
and ranking; ROLL owns deploy presentation and release selection.

### 20.1 Prepared source and selection

Deploy compares every selectable ordinary predecessor, even without sampled
widened rows. A widening without an applicable authored down requires a compiled
rollback view. All needed views must prepare successfully or deploy refuses.
View admission compares structural source domains (including text minima and
formats), nominal aliases, unique keys and caps; it never parses human advice
strings to decide whether a view is needed. A fitting authored scalar down
removes its covered value-domain risk; extraction/renames retain their typed
identity laws.
The compatible join is LLP 3000.000.000.002 §8: optional union, integer and text
bound union with identical format, enum union, recursively joined bounded lists,
and asset size-bound union with identical nominal kind, MIME and once policy.
Nullability and list cardinality join separately. This differs from extraction,
which requires full type equality, including size, optionality and cardinality.
References join only across explicit nominal identity renames. A mixed change
cannot enlarge any current shared-column domain with predecessor-only values:
current read/write predicates may rely on their schema-level impossibility.
The ordinary union view refuses even if that column is not the widening trigger.
A closed alternative may retain the complete current authored source closure
(schema, defaults, programs, helpers, jobs and authority), rather than synthesize
a union. It requires every predecessor table and column under the same spelling,
every predecessor operation name/kind, and current argument domains accepting
every predecessor input under the same closed compatibility rule below. It
retains the exact current domains and values, with no resurrection, translation
or old-only writes. Missing fields/names or incompatible inputs refuse. Current
result semantics are disclosed for old-UI review; current extra operations
remain current-authorized. Native/worker bundle qualification remains mandatory.
Otherwise author a compatible source and truthful migration before deploying. Enum order
and nominal rename spelling do not change this domain comparison. Domain
includes nullability, recursive list-element/cardinality bounds, text formats,
integer bounds, asset kind/size/MIME/once constraints; unsupported or incompatible
domain changes refuse rather than truncate or coerce. No conversion,
zero-money fallback, implicit business default or arbitrary row choice is inferred.

The view retains predecessor operation names and argument contracts, including
existing authored operation aliases under their compilation law (same-module,
no alias chains). Every exported predecessor alias counts as an old name; a
current alias resolves to its actual authored target for input, guard, helper
and authority comparison. Renaming an export never drops its current guards. A current
require can be copied only with identical argument and entire transitive local
binding/helper meanings, lookup predicates and control-flow paths. A changed
unmodeled closure or guard depending on writes refuses. Canonical expression
comparison ignores source locations, never semantics. When a relaxed unique key invalidates an old lookup, or current guards require
an added field on a shared table, retain that field and its authored current
backfill. An affected operation whose current guard/workflow cannot be transported
unchanged may likewise reuse its current authored endpoint under the same closed
input-compatibility rule. Every current added field is retained on a shared table, even without a guard
mention; required fields retain their authored default/backfill and every old
write path must supply them through a compatible current authored endpoint or
refuse compilation. No missing-required-field write is permitted. All helpers in a reused workflow, including write,
return and system-scope dependencies, need identical transitive meanings. An already-authored current endpoint of the same name/kind whose
arguments accept every predecessor input may replace the affected old endpoint;
the initial comparison permits identical argument types, optional widening and
integer-bound widening and enum-superset widening, never narrowing or new required arguments. Retained
current backfill identity keeps its output custody active. This supplies authored
writes for the added required fields instead of inventing defaults; the generator never invents first-row
selection. The replacement's point/limited selection must retain the language's
deterministic total ordering, including its stable id tie-break; a source outside
that deterministic language fragment refuses. Missing or incompatible authored
endpoints refuse compilation.

A generated visible view retains the current forward-migration declaration set
for shared output columns. It does not resurrect a retired predecessor transform
whose outputs already remain current columns in the compiled union. Historical
source and receipts remain intact. This preserves current native values and
migration custody instead of reinterpreting them with a retired input domain.
Transforms needed for fields removed from current source remain subject to the
ordinary truthful-projection and complete-adoption checks.

A generated visible view omits a predecessor-only historical down declaration
when all its outputs remain current columns whose generated domains admit the
current native values. Its immutable original declaration and receipt evidence
remain historical; the view does not reenter that retired projection or restore
stale preimages over native values. Downs needed for removed fields remain.
Exact original-source selection retains its projection/conflict behavior. Writer
adoption still enforces full domains, lifetimes and zero-unacknowledged hiding.

Shared tables retain current caps, immutability, final-existence constraints
and lifecycle guards. Generated source carries authored current constraints,
unique keys, expiry and opening declarations; the compiled view must preserve
current hard constraints, reference-deletion/lifetime shape and sync audience
or preparation refuses. Text constraints follow the explicit domain join, not
a separate implicit relaxation. Schema quotas cannot become weaker merely
because their current rule IR is unchanged. Unsupported structural transport
requires an authored compatible workflow; no arbitrary population choice or
new business default is generated.

Shared tables retain the **current authored authority**, including transition
bindings, read rules, payload guards and field ownership. The compiled bound IR
must equal current authority after declared identity alignment. A release switch
is a view over the same current authoritative rows, never restoration of a
historical database snapshot: deleted rows stay deleted and current values
stay current except declared truthful migrations. No resurrection or replay of
historical preimages over authored current images is authorized. With no
hiding and no domain enlarging beyond current, those row populations remain
the current populations; changing read visibility still follows exact current
authority.
For intentional hiding, IR equality alone is insufficient: §20.2 additionally
checks population-sensitive authority/guards under the actual writer. Removed old immutability/uniqueness
constraints follow explicitly relaxed current constraints. Newer-only tables
retain current rules without promising old clients expose new concepts. Native
or worker dependencies without a qualified authored bundle refuse. Undefined
fields, changed helper closures and incompatible workflows refuse rather than
weakening guards. Nullable result fields and carried/reused guards appear in
an action-focused checklist; successful mechanical adaptations are facts, not
11–42 mandatory decisions. The commissioning author's chosen visible-rollback
contract explicitly permits nullable/wider result domains; these are disclosed
as RESULT compatibility changes, never claimed to retain a nonnull v1 result
contract. Requiring nonnull results would contradict that requested end game.
Names and accepted input contracts remain preserved. Dormant restoration advice
names --restore-dormant.

Removing a defaulted predecessor column requires an authored truthful down.
The initial grammar has no acknowledged-absence escape for removed defaults.
Preview may suggest a cross-table expression from declared references/columns,
but it is advisory source for the author to review, never an inferred business
mapping. A false default is not a reversible representation.

Prepared source is immutable. Each parent manifest maps original target id to
numeric view id, executable digest, changed columns, carried guards and hiding
reason. The parent's full control digest includes the manifest; the view's full
control digest includes its metadata. Prepared views are usable only after the
parent's CAS activation commits and never count as ordinary latest or equal
forward source. Failed parents remove stages/views. Unknown admitted outcomes
retain them for history-authoritative recovery; uncommitted parents cannot
authorize selection. Before either equal-source no-op or retained-source reactivation, prepare the
complete desired view set privately and compare target/executable digest/hiding
reasons with that source's own committed manifest, verifying its entries. A
matching set permits no-op or identity reuse. A missing, stale or newly needed
mapping creates an ordinary registration parent using the same source and full
adoption/CAS validation; no view is selectable before that commit. Output and
JSON identify the equivalent retained source as sourceRelease. Source and old
manifests remain immutable. Temporary desired views are removed on no-op/reuse,
refusal and preview. This also upgrades unchanged legacy deployments without
needed registrations. Later equal source is a no-op once its desired set matches.
Retained-source reactivation and rollback preserve their own registry context.

Resolve the manifest belonging to the active ordinary release, or the
registered parent of the active compiled view, under the command lock. Never
borrow a later deployment's view across source branches: it may carry weaker
guards than the active source. If that context has no mapping, default selection
uses original source with the same writer visibility checks. Bind actual source
digest, expected release and activation revision to the existing CAS. ABA/equal-content switches change revision. Cold, hot,
preview and restart use that actual immutable source. Selection restores dormant
names implicitly, with full data/constraint/lifetime validation. Output and
releases identify original and actual source. --exact selects the original and
reports dormant hiding; it bypasses only default visible-view selection, never
applicable security/strict refusals, authority checks or migration validation.

### 20.2 Writer-enforced visibility

Default rollback, even without a registered view, admits zero unacknowledged
hidden predecessor-table rows. Candidate preview also refuses uncovered hiding
on reverse snapshots. Future bad projections refuse selection under the writer.
Preview populations are privileged retained-row counts, not viewer guarantees.

The initial intentional-hiding spelling is:
`rollback N hides TABLE where COLUMN = null because 'REASON'`.
The candidate column must be nullable, the predecessor column nonnullable, and
table/column spellings identical. N is the immutable original. No arbitrary
predicate, blanket exemption or rename combination is admitted. Its view keeps
the null restriction while joining the nonnull domain: nullable 0..1000 versus
required 0..100 becomes required 0..1000, preserving widened nonnull values.
The nonempty reason travels in immutable metadata and repeats on selection.

Under the actual writer transaction, capture the before image, run full adoption,
then compare every retained hidden predecessor row. Only acknowledged null rows
and the actual hidden-reference closure rooted in them are allowed; unrelated
hidden rows refuse atomically. The closure graph contains only scalar/optional
nominal Ref cells in before-image active rows, as typed by the source schema;
ids are aligned through the catalog identity. An edge qualifies only when its
parent was also active before, is actually retained-hidden after adoption, and
has no invalid reference-lifetime marker. No list, synthetic extraction anchor,
asset, dormant overlay, cascade or set-null declaration invents an edge. A
renamed identity uses its same catalog number. Before-hidden rows are outside
this newly-hidden population; newer-only tables are distinguished. The snapshot
preview is advisory; concurrent writes cannot bypass activation revalidation.
Acknowledgment never changes physical preservation or permits broader read/write
authority. After collecting the actual newly hidden table set, the writer rejects
negative existence authority, reference comparisons that can admit absence, or
population-tainted requirements/control flow. Positive existence requirements
may only become denials. A direct point assertion may discharge absence taint:
if its row is hidden it aborts before subsequent work; its surviving field values
remain unchanged. Writes to affected existing targets require that same exact
point-presence guard. Transitive bindings and compiler-expanded helper bodies
are followed; unknown paths refuse. Tainted branch/loop/conditional-write keys AND persisted write values,
negative/count-based guard dependencies, nested job-query scope and maintained
populations depending on hidden sources are conservatively outside this initial
acknowledgment form. This is a closed syntactic admission check, not an inferred
semantic implication or a finite-snapshot security proof. Explicit reasons do
not bypass it. Source may retain the safety witness in a visible bridge instead.
Ordinary hidden-row occupancy/retention laws remain explicit in the preview;
intentional hiding supplies no promise of automatic forward conflict resolution.

Point-presence proofs discharge taint only for subsequent work in program
order on that exact table/key. A guard cannot justify any earlier tainted read
or write. Mutations/jobs retain their existing atomic transaction law: a later
refusal rolls back all prior writes in the operation, including work before a
point assertion. The population checker does not weaken that transaction law.

### 20.3 Edited transformation outputs

An edited in-place migration output reverses through an applicable down on its
assembled current image, or stays exact if the compiled selected view's full
type/constraints accept that physical value. This never widens a schema per row,
coerces, truncates, null-fills or invents defaults. Otherwise selection refuses.
Original preimage, actual authored newer output, prior computation and physical
old-view presence are separate evidence; edited output never gains native birth.

Forward validates old-view field presence/null/default/lifetime evidence first.
Preserving authored output requires unchanged explicit arguments AND freshly
unchanged computation, including point/existence lookups. Changed inputs require
exact deterministic agreement with authored output or a real conflict printing
competing values. Unedited outputs retain preimage precedence; receipts state
when older views read stale until forward. Repeated and partially retired cycles,
invalid lifetimes, pruned evidence and meter cuts obey the same atomic laws.

### 20.4 Reference renames and asset extraction

Reference renamed-from transports the existing physical cell and reference
lifetime bindings, including retained/narrowed images. It never recaptures a
deleted/reused parent or captured/engine-owned field. Both aliases at once and
unequal destination cells refuse. After identity alignment, nominal target,
nullability, bounds, on-delete and capture shape must be exactly equal; widening
is a separate migration, not part of this rename.

`extract CHILD from PARENT via REFERENCE: {newField: oldField}` is a declarative
migration, not an operation or cross-table rename. It creates one receipt-owned
initial child per source row; child's deterministic id equals parent id in the
child namespace. The child has a required reference to parent with cascade or
restrict deletion; keep/set-null are outside the initial form. Independent active
or dormant destination history refuses. At least one mapped asset is required,
with identical full source/child type. Parent must remove that source asset
column. Other copied fields must be explicitly immutable scalar source fields;
mutable, captured, maintained and nominal-reference copies refuse. Copies must
continue to agree both directions. Other required child columns need authored
defaults. Receipt identity uses numeric parent/child catalog identities and declared column
origins, so table, asset-column and uncaptured reference-column aliases do not
create a new migration or recapture an anchor. Typed schema normalization updates
extraction table/reference slots and preserves source/destination column strings.
Destination admission inspects active rows, dormant rows/cells and competing
receipts. Reentry requires its existing receipt-owned child and valid anchor;
pruned/deleted child history is never recreated from metadata.

Future independent children with distinct ids are allowed; extraction is not
unique-parent. Existing durable id reservation and duplicate-key admission apply
to receipt-owned/dormant children exactly as to ordinary rows. The normal mint
never overwrites an occupied id; an explicitly forced collision refuses. Neither
rollback nor deleting receipt metadata frees a still-retained row id.

Asset ledger home and physical asset cell move together, without reuploading or
changing bytes. The former active cell and equal dormant overlay are removed;
a distinct overlay refuses rather than being discarded. Private displaced-cell
receipt evidence persists separately from active cells. Reverse removes the
child asset cell while retaining identity/scalars and newer-only children. Later
forward uses the current old-view asset, including replacement/optional clearing,
never reviving released receipt bytes or creating a second ledger home.

A native child without a receipt reverses only when exactly one total candidate child
exists for that parent, and that child has a valid private parent-lifetime anchor. Anchors are captured while
extraction is active, including absent-parent invalid anchors; unchanged writes
never recapture invalid evidence. Older independent relations cannot qualify
retroactively. Deletion/id reuse, reparenting and ambiguity refuse; no arbitrary
photo selection. Receipt-owned children likewise require valid lifetime evidence.

For row-dependent audiences the child must read through its parent with identical
parent policy; otherwise source/child read policies must be identical and row-
independent, or destination denies reads. Existing payload gates must match.
Reverse must preserve the current effective child/parent/column audience or
refuse with guarded-bridge advice. These are closed admission rules, not a
policy implication prover. Native/ref compatibility does not confer byte access.

Actual activation revalidates active AND dormant destination emptiness, regular
byte presence/ledger length, full types, scalar copies, parent binding, anchor
validity and reverse ambiguity inside adoption under command locking and CAS.
Preview runs this transaction on a private copy; its counts cannot authorize a
later activation. Failure rolls back evidence, format, homes, selection and
generation together. Devices execute no migration metadata or home authority:
authenticated serving resolves the current server ledger home and current row/
column policy. Existing device generation and media-retention receipts remain;
this introduces no retroactive cache-erasure claim.

Store format 9 fences edited-output/reference/extraction AND compiled-view
registry custody from older runtimes. Every successful release adoption upgrades
to 9 transactionally, before prepared registry entries become selectable. Older
CLIs must not mistake view ids for ordinary latest or skip visibility policy.
Every hot activation request from this CLI carries the explicit registry-custody
feature field, including exact/original-source selection, an empty registry and
an existing format-8 store. The field covers all format-9 adoption laws, including
edited outputs; it is not conditional on a visible view. Updated hosts reject
activation clients omitting it. Older hosts reject that unknown field before
admission; restart serve with the updated runtime. Updated hosts always require
it before activation admission, with no conditional old-wire-shape exception.
This prevents an older engine from executing any adoption without the new laws.
Unchanged no-op deployments without new custody can stay at 8. Format 8 opens;
failed adoption leaves its old format unchanged; 9 survives
old-source selection. Capabilities release-child-extraction-v1 and
release-reference-renames-v1 fence schema admission. Device projections omit
migration metadata while retaining ordinary current tables and through policies.

### 20.5 Presentation and qualification

Human deploy output starts with verdict/security FIX/test status, followed by
adoption/rollback facts. Result advice groups a changed source column with its
affected operations once. Security review is last, scoped to changed declarations,
ranked and capped at five advisory notes, with omitted count. Reachable FIX
witnesses are never capped/suppressed. A trailing verdict supports tail readers.
JSON carries every compatibility detail, report, test status and view mapping.
Campaign matching/stale/missing/incomplete state and age remain explicit. Finite
tests never certify universal security. SEC7 owns ordinary check ranking/gates.

Readiness requires blind Muse and fresh Codex READY reviews of the same revision,
retained verbatim with provenance. Qualification covers cycles/conflicts, optional
money, image/file homes, deleted/reused references, view failure/CAS/restart,
bakeoff copies and packed guide cases. This record claims no unrun test or
publication and authorizes no push/version bump.

## 2. Language

A top-level declaration describes one table's historical input fields and its
simultaneous output patch:

```quarry
backfill bookings vehicleClass(weight: int, length: int):
  { vehicleClass: 'commercial' if weight > 3500 else 'van' if length >= 500 else 'car' }
```

Here length is stored in centimetres. Arguments are typed historical fields;
they remain in the declaration after the candidate drops those fields. The
compiler binds arguments against their explicit types, not today's table.
The runtime validates them against each interpreted historical row. Output
names belong to candidate authoritative columns and may equal input names:
read-old/write-new. Outputs of multiple declarations on a table must be
disjoint. Names are unique per table. Durable store history keeps one
normalized definition per table/name, including gaps; changing it refuses with a
bridge/new-name repair. A new name denotes a new transformation.

The expression body is an object patch. It uses Quarry's existing arithmetic,
comparisons, null handling, conditional expressions, immutable helper expansion,
and bounded deterministic scalar builtins. Supported text additions are
`before(text, delimiter)`, `after`, `beforeLast`, `afterLast`, `startsWith`,
and `allDigits`. Splits remove the first/last exact delimiter; absent delimiter
means whole text before, empty text after. An empty split delimiter refuses.
Digits means nonempty ASCII digits. Text is Unicode, comparisons and splits
are locale independent. Existing `lower`, `trim`, `slice`, `count`, `sha256`,
`first`, `last`, `at`, `min`, `max`, exact `divide`, calendar/civil transforms
and concatenation retain their normal checked semantics. No randomness,
viewer, clock, minted ids, writes, mutation helpers or loops are admitted.
Identity `id` is never an output. Only scalar outputs are admitted, including new references. Assets, lists,
engine-owned/captured/maintained and lifetime outputs refuse, including fields
owned by a historical source lifetime even when the candidate removes that rule.
Restored narrowed rows retain their individual output ownership, captures and
reference types. Columns added by later predecessor schemas contribute their
own types/defaults only when absent from that archived definition. Overwriting an
existing reference/asset is refused: derive a new reference column so the
existing dormant reference-lifetime ledger owns retirement/restoration.

Point reads use the ordinary `members[principal = owner] ! else NOT_FOUND`
spelling. They require a unique point. A missing asserted point refuses;
ordinary nullable point reads and `exists` retain their explicit semantics.
Historical existence predicates may traverse a metered source range, needed
for wave-17 cross-tower references; they return no row collection. A meter
cut is a refusal, never false absence or an incomplete historical value.
Before evaluating any patch, each unique lookup index validates the historical
population's cardinality with bounded privileged duplicate samples. A different
simultaneous patch making candidate keys unique cannot choose an arbitrary old
match. All reads run as the trusted activation system principal, bypassing application
read rules, against the assembled historical snapshot. Dormant namespaces
remain unnameable. Candidate read indexes are built over that source snapshot
only when their component fields exist there. No JavaScript or app migration
operation receives this authority. All declarations are self-contained: they
cannot read another declaration's outputs in the same activation.

## 3. Adoption and reversibility

The existing activation writer transaction owns this sequence:

1. Assemble retained source rows/cells and their source definitions using the
   existing restoration intent. This includes narrowed rows eligible to return:
   restore their full historical images before evaluation, deferring only the
   backfill output columns' candidate-type checks. Retained reference invalidation,
   source lifetime and audience checks still apply before restoration. Retire
   source-expired lifetimes before any backfill; a transform cannot extend an
   expiry or opening boundary.
2. When leaving a declaration, restore its predecessor-visible output fields
   from saved **output preimages**, separately from its argument inputs. Save
   interpreted old values, including literal defaults/nulls, and absence.
   Added output columns use ordinary dormant retention instead. A different
   current value from both recorded output and preimage refuses with a conflict;
   untouched same-column enum renames restore their exact original value.
   Membership is per row lifetime, with normalized declaration definitions kept
   beside evidence. Removing a whole table suspends its membership rather than
   feeding transformed values back as inputs. Re-entering that declaration reads
   its original overwritten inputs and preserves its current result; selecting
   original source instead reverses it even when the immediately previous schema
   lacked the table/declaration. Ordinary active deletion clears membership.
3. Enter declarations absent from the immediately active schema. Unchanged
   carry-forward and reopening run none for ordinary active rows: candidate-native
   writes are not historical rows. Newly restored narrowed rows receive carried
   declarations too, including a declaration introduced while they were dormant. On re-entry, restored cells/rows are eligible along with
   older active rows. A native v2 row whose absent historical inputs have a
   complete valid restored output image keeps that image. Older v1 rows lacking
   outputs are filled. Declarations must accompany skipped-release source;
   no unseen intermediate transformation is implicitly inferred.
4. Evaluate every entering patch against one assembled source snapshot, with
   ordinary per-row job meters and a paged source walk. Stage output/evidence
   in SQLite temp storage before applying any patch. Recompute the patch on
   re-entry, so changed lookup values count as changed dependencies even when
   declared arguments are unchanged. Compare both saved argument values and
   computed outputs. If unchanged, preserve edited added-column cells. If
   changed, recompute only when current outputs equal the saved result or the
   reversed preimage; otherwise refuse the conflict. Never overwrite a manual
   output edit because another input happened to change. A fully reversed
   same-column output edited during absence also refuses re-entry when its
   current interpreted value equals neither preimage nor saved result, even
   with unchanged arguments. Sparse defaults count by interpreted value.
5. Apply patches and evidence together. Run ordinary narrowing/reference
   closure, projection, complete type/asset validation, indexes, uniqueness,
   caps and maintained-data rebuilds. Transformed dependencies force rebuilds
   even when their declarations did not change. Publish programs, generation
   and release in the same commit. Any error rolls everything back.

Evidence is keyed by table number, row lifetime and the SHA-256 digest of the
normalized declaration/typed IR (no diagnostic locations or whitespace).
It records declared input values, each overwritten output preimage and computed
output. Ordinary authoritative deletion/expiry deletes that row's evidence in
its transaction; id reuse cannot inherit it. Row moves to dormant storage
preserve lifetime. Backfill definitions enter schema/activation identity;
changed rows also set activation population, forcing a fresh generation and
device reacquisition. Public/device schema projections omit all backfill
metadata; devices never execute migrations. A successful evidence installation
sets `snapback4-release-store-6`, transactionally. Unaware binaries refuse that
format even when an old source is selected. Failed installation retains the
previous format. Legacy stores without backfills remain unchanged. Prune previews
backfill preimages separately, with counts, bytes and bounded ids. Explicit
prune deletes their values and keeps only per-lifetime reversal-unavailable
identities. Leaving an overwritten column then refuses with a pruned-preimage
reason: select a bridge retaining present meaning or restore a backup. Active
row deletion clears these identities too. Pruning added-column evidence does
not prevent rollback when no old-visible field needs a preimage.

Dropping a declaration has explicit semantic meaning: leave it by restoring
preimages or refusing conflicts. Keep declarations in every later source
whose view should retain their meaning. Refusals include exact affected counts
and up to five bounded privileged samples. Missing inputs/lookups, output/type
errors, arithmetic/meter errors and uniqueness/cap conflicts refuse atomically;
a widened/optional non-unique bridge plus an authorized repair mutation is the
repair path. A post-backfill-edit conflict requires keeping the current/bridge
source, deciding a truthful old representation, then repairing it before
selecting old source. No automatic fallback turns a new enum value into an old
meaning. Native v2 rows with no preimage retain the parent's existing law:
forward selection may make incompatible present values dormant; rollback
requires representability and refuses with its existing reason. This amendment
does not change REL5's rollback or restoration selection policy.

`deploy --check` uses the existing private disk snapshot, deploy direction and
restoration intent; rollback previews use rollback direction. Later live writes
can change its plan. `backfills` in both cold/hot JSON receipts and exact preview
text reports `table name transformed N rows` or `table name reversed on N rows`.
These are transformed row visits, separate from the parent's disjoint dormant
rows/cells/index entries. They are not additive counts of distinct rows across
different transformations, and do not claim a frozen production population.

Consumed historical references must retain valid parent-lifetime evidence even
when the input column is dropped. Expired predecessor/restored images are retired
before any transform, including ordinary expiry without read-through. Pruning
keeps membership-only tombstones solely for surviving lifetimes; such a tombstone
refuses re-entry requiring lost values rather than treating the row as fresh.

Re-entry retains an adoption-cohort identity with each active row receipt.
All declarations first applied in one activation, including continuously carried
declarations on tables that were never removed, read one reconstructed source
snapshot, including point/range indexes; this overlays that cohort's complete
input evidence and output preimages (including original absence) on current
images. Distinct original cohorts are evaluated separately against their
respective historical overlays, so a declaration introduced later can read an
earlier declaration's already-transformed values. Computed and retained/native output values must fit candidate scalar types and
text minimum/format constraints before narrowing; invalid generated outputs
refuse the entire adoption with affected-row samples. Patches remain staged until
all cohorts succeed, and current row images are restored before applying them.
New declarations use the current assembled predecessor snapshot. Inactive
cohorts restore their own still-omitted input interpretations, independently of
new declarations in that activation, and catch up changed authored inputs. Declared historical arguments consume retained
cells and source definitions even when the candidate omits those columns;
retained parent-lifetime bindings still apply. A native-image exception requires a birth marker for this exact declaration,
a matching fingerprint of the original non-output inputs, and valid, complete outputs;
a new declaration never skips valid old inputs merely because an older
transformation left a retained output. Receipt reference inputs use private namespaced bindings in the existing
dormant parent-lifetime ledger, independently of the live input column. A
retargeted/dropped input cannot substitute its current binding for the saved
reference. Every unchanged saved argument consumed during active or inactive
re-entry validates its private lifetime binding before program evaluation.
Bindings use each restored narrowed row's actual archived reference type
(rather than the table-wide predecessor), even when an
argument is declared as generic `id`. Existing private bindings survive
re-entry with unchanged saved inputs even after the visible column becomes
generic `id`; schema type erasure cannot recapture the same parent id. Reconstruction validates any consumed saved binding before restoring
its value. Original absence is recorded separately from the predecessor's interpreted
value. A physically missing field consumes its source default when present,
otherwise null; receipts retain that interpretation explicitly, preventing
later defaults from changing argument or lookup/index evaluation. Expired sources retire when entering or leaving the
last declaration, including restored table images.

When transformed rows become dormant, candidate output reference types supply
parent bindings even when the predecessor had no such column. Existing invalid
bindings are preserved. If a restored row becomes dormant again, only its
explicitly backfilled authoritative cells update the retained image; original
source definition, other cells and authority provenance stay intact. Forward
selection after rollback reconstructs absent added outputs for that same
declaration before evaluating its arguments, rather than feeding it its own
retained result.

Input omission is stored independently from interpreted null/default values,
both for the current physical lifetime and for each receipt. Ordinary writes
compare interpreted old/new inputs; meaningful edits end omission provenance,
while unrelated writes and default materialization do not. Receipts reconstruct
their original interpretation of omitted fields across later default changes.

Rows inserted while a declaration is active receive a private native-birth
marker keyed by its normalized definition and a hash of their input image;
no original input values are kept in that marker. The same declaration preserves
complete native images with unchanged inputs, including absent optional inputs,
constant transforms and whole-table restoration. Changed native inputs, invalid
present inputs, lost native output images or conflicting other-transform history
refuse with a bridge reason. An inactive overlapping receipt is compatible only
when every overlapping current output equals its recorded preimage; an active
or pruned conflicting history still refuses. Same-column output values belong
to the candidate type, rather than the historical input type, for native rows.
A new declaration name does not inherit another
name's birth marker. Prune retains only native fingerprints for surviving
lifetimes, alongside current omission flags; receipt absence flags are forgotten
with historical values. Delete/reset remove every marker for the ended lifetime.

Source retirement uses the ordinary engine expiry/reference-action closure
under the complete assembled predecessor schema, before any patch runs or
removed table is archived. Only surviving post-action rows enter dormant storage. Restored source
indexes and cap occupancy are rebuilt first; writing-reference groups must fit
the source reservation's live bound. Cascade and set-null effects participate
in this same transaction; restrict or retained-child blockers refuse the entire
adoption. Retired source population forces a new generation so clients cannot
keep children removed by a cascade. Authoritative set-null updates corresponding archived cells/narrowed images
to null before clearing old column bindings, while receipt input bindings remain
independent. An expired
narrowed source is exposed only inside this uncommitted lifecycle phase and
cannot reach a backfill evaluation alive.

Development reset still empties every lifetime's data/history/native/omission
markers, then reconstructs code-derived declaration/input registries from its
retained schema before reseeding; the new identity's rows receive new birth
markers. No release HTTP reset door is added.

Evidence decode is preflighted against
the existing job heap ceiling. Text preview lists private preimages separately
from dormant application rows/cells/index entries.

### Per-column transformed views

A receipt records a `retained_outputs` set independently of its declaration's
active flag. When leaving a declaration, candidate-visible transformed outputs
reverse to their preimages. Outputs absent from the candidate remain transformed
in dormant storage; their set membership survives until reversal or re-entry.
Even an inactive declaration reverses those suspended fields when a later plain
schema exposes them. Whole-table suspension keeps the transformed view intact.

Re-entry preserves the saved preimage for each still-transformed output. A
fully reversed output instead captures its actual assembled source value,
including restored dormant cells and interpreted predecessor defaults, before
applying the patch. Input history and rollback preimages are separate: an input
may retain its original omitted/null interpretation while the latest rollback
view has a literal default. Ordinary writes do not erase omission provenance
when they edit a transformed output from that same original cohort.

An inverse restoring genuine absence records a transaction-local source mask.
Later evaluation cannot fill that absence from the suspended result or a default
belonging to the output schema. The mask is dropped on every exit. Earlier
recorded cohorts' staged patches are replayed into later recorded source views;
fresh declarations still share the untouched predecessor snapshot and cannot
read each other's newly staged outputs. Every decode remains heap-admitted.

Reference arguments copy any matching ordinary cell-lifetime bindings before
restoration consumes that ledger, including transformed reference cells absent
from an archived whole-row definition. Unchanged saved inputs retain existing
private bindings; inferred current bindings cannot overwrite their invalidation.
The stable format is `snapback4-release-store-6`, with schema capability
`release-backfills-v3`. Published 0.4.33's store format 3 upgrades on first use.
Unpublished experimental formats 4/5 and the older capabilities cannot silently read
this evidence layout. Package versions are not changed in this workstream.

    43	fn anchored(
    44	    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    45	    ct: i64,
    46	    child_id: &str,
    47	    key: &str,
    48	    pt: i64,
    49	    parent_id: &str,
    50	) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
    51	    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_refs WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3 AND parent_t=?4 AND parent_id=?5 AND invalid=0)",params![ct,child_id,format!("$extract:{key}"),pt,parent_id],|r|r.get(0))?)

    53	fn remember(
    54	    tx: &Tx<'_>,
    55	    key: &str,
    56	    pt: i64,
    57	    id: &str,
    58	    column: &str,
    59	    v: &Value,
    60	    meter: &mut Meter,
    61	) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    62	    meter.heap(v.retained_heap_bytes().saturating_mul(4).saturating_add(8192))?;
    63	    let encoded = canonical_json(v);
    64	    let conflicting:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values d WHERE d.t=?1 AND d.id=?2 AND d.column_name=?3 AND d.data!=?4 AND NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _extraction_cells e WHERE e.definition=?5 AND e.parent_t=d.t AND e.parent_id=d.id AND e.column_name=d.column_name AND e.data=d.data))",params![pt,id,column,encoded,key],|r|r.get(0))?;
    65	    if conflicting {
    66	            return Err(refuse(
    67	                "extract each has a conflicting retained source list; preserve both meanings in a bridge",
    68	            ));
    69	    }
    70	    tx.conn().execute("INSERT INTO _extraction_cells VALUES(?1,?2,?3,?4,?5) ON CONFLICT(definition,parent_t,parent_id,column_name) DO UPDATE SET data=excluded.data",params![key,pt,id,column,encoded])?;
    71	    tx.conn().execute(
    72	        "DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",
    73	        params![pt, id, column],
    74	    )?;
    75	    Ok(())
    76	}

    91	// A migration-owned null can remain in narrowed storage across old views.
    92	// Reading it validates custody without restoring it into the active population.
    93	fn receipt_image(tx: &Tx<'_>, ct: i64, id: &str, meter:&mut Meter) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
    94	    read_image(tx,ct,id,meter,true)
    95	}
    96	fn read_image(tx: &Tx<'_>, ct: i64, id: &str, meter:&mut Meter, dormant:bool) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
    97	    let item:Option<(i64,bool)>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT octet_length(data),1 FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 UNION ALL SELECT octet_length(data),0 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND ?3 LIMIT 1",params![ct,id,dormant],|r|Ok((r.get(0)?,r.get(1)?))).optional()?;
    98	    let Some((size,active))=item else { return Ok(None) };
    99	    let scratch=meter.mark();
   100	    meter.heap((size.max(0) as u64).saturating_mul(64).saturating_add(8192))?;
   101	    let sql=if active {"SELECT data FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2"} else {"SELECT data FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2"};
   102	    let text:String=tx.conn().query_row(sql,params![ct,id],|r|r.get(0))?;
   103	    let image:Value=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|refuse(e.to_string()))?;
   104	    drop(text);
   105	    meter.release_heap(scratch);
   106	    // Decoder scratch is gone; the decoded image and its write/projection
   107	    // scratch remain resident alongside the walker's page and prior children.
   108	    meter.heap(image.retained_heap_bytes().saturating_mul(4).saturating_add(8192))?;
   109	    Ok(Some(image))
   110	}
   111	fn read_value(tx:&Tx<'_>,t:i64,id:&str,row:&Value,column:&str,meter:&mut Meter)->Result<Option<Value>,RuntimeError>{
   112	    if let Ok(value)=row.field(column) {
   113	        // Includes the retained value, list bookkeeping and scalar-copy scratch.
   114	        meter.heap(value.retained_heap_bytes().saturating_mul(4).saturating_add(8192))?;
   115	        return Ok(Some(value.clone()));
   116	    }
   117	    let bytes:Option<i64>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT octet_length(data) FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",params![t,id,column],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   118	    let Some(bytes)=bytes else{return Ok(None)};
   119	    let scratch=meter.mark();
   120	    meter.heap((bytes.max(0) as u64).saturating_mul(64).saturating_add(8192))?;
   121	    let text:String=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT data FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",params![t,id,column],|r|r.get(0))?;
   122	    let value:Value=serde_json::from_str(&text).map_err(|e|refuse(e.to_string()))?;
   123	    drop(text);meter.release_heap(scratch);
   124	    meter.heap(value.retained_heap_bytes().saturating_mul(4).saturating_add(8192))?;
   125	    Ok(Some(value))
   126	}
   127	fn validate_receipts(tx:&Tx<'_>,key:&str,pt:i64,id:&str,ct:i64,meter:&mut Meter)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   128	    let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT octet_length(child_id),child_id FROM _extraction_each_rows WHERE definition=?1 AND parent_t=?2 AND parent_id=?3 ORDER BY child_id")?;
   129	    let mut receipts=q.query(params![key,pt,id])?;
   130	    while let Some(record)=receipts.next()? {
   131	        meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined,1)?;
   132	        let scratch=meter.mark();
   133	        let size:i64=record.get(0)?;
   134	        meter.heap((size.max(0) as u64).saturating_mul(8).saturating_add(128))?;
   135	        let child:String=record.get(1)?;
   136	        if receipt_image(tx,ct,&child,meter)?.is_none() || !anchored(tx,ct,&child,key,pt,id)? {
   137	            return Err(refuse("returning extract each lacks its receipt-owned child or valid parent-lifetime evidence; never recreate pruned/deleted history"));
   138	        }
   139	        drop(child);meter.release_heap(scratch);
   140	    }
   141	    Ok(())
   142	}

   144	pub(super) fn source<'a>(e:&'a Extraction,child:&Table)->&'a str { mapping(e,child).1 }
   145	pub(super) fn known(tx:&Tx<'_>,key:&str)->Result<bool,RuntimeError>{
   146	    setup(tx)?;
   147	    Ok(tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _extraction_each_definitions WHERE definition=?1)",[key],|r|r.get(0))?)
   148	}
   149	fn validate_all_receipts(tx:&Tx<'_>,key:&str,pt:i64,ct:i64)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   150	    let mut q=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT DISTINCT octet_length(parent_id),parent_id FROM _extraction_each_rows WHERE definition=?1 AND parent_t=?2 AND child_t=?3 ORDER BY parent_id")?;
   151	    let mut parents=q.query(params![key,pt,ct])?;
   152	    while let Some(record)=parents.next()? {
   153	        let mut meter=Meter::new(Ceilings::job(),Cut::Abort);
   154	        let size:i64=record.get(0)?;
   155	        meter.heap((size.max(0) as u64).saturating_mul(8).saturating_add(128))?;
   156	        let id:String=record.get(1)?;
   157	        validate_receipts(tx,key,pt,&id,ct,&mut meter)?;
   158	    }
   159	    Ok(())
   160	}
   161	// A suspended declaration has already consumed its historical list. Resume
   162	// validates current child custody; it never replays stale list preimages.
   163	pub(super) fn resume(tx:&Tx<'_>,e:&Extraction,parent:&Table,child:&Table,key:&str,report:&mut super::super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport)->Result<(),RuntimeError>{
   164	    if !known(tx,key)? { return Err(refuse("extract each source list is absent and no successfully applied extraction owns this suspended definition")); }
   165	    let pt=tx.num(&e.parent)?;let ct=tx.num(&e.child)?;
   166	    validate_all_receipts(tx,key,pt,ct)?;
   167	    let (destination,_)=mapping(e,child);let mut count=0u64;
   168	    super::super::activation_walk::walk_metered(tx,ct,crate::rows::adoption::Work::DECODE,None,|id,image,meter|{
   169	        let parent_id=image.field(&e.reference)?.as_str()?;
   170	        if !anchored(tx,ct,id,key,pt,parent_id)? { return Err(refuse("resuming extract each lacks valid captured parent-lifetime evidence; never retrospectively capture an independent child")); }
   171	        let active:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2)",params![pt,parent_id],|r|r.get(0))?;
   172	        if !active {return Err(refuse("resuming extract each requires its existing current parent; preserve narrowed parent authority in a bridge"));}
   173	        let current=receipt_image(tx,pt,parent_id,meter)?.ok_or_else(||refuse("resuming extract each lost its current parent"))?;
   174	        if let Some(through)=&child.through {
   175	            let current=crate::schema::row_image(&current,parent);
   176	            if through.fields.iter().any(|(capture,field)|image.field(capture).ok()!=current.field(field).ok()) { return Err(refuse("resuming extract each would change captured parent authority; use an authored bridge")); }
   177	        }
   178	        for(dest,src)in &e.fields {
   179	            if dest!=destination && image.field(dest).ok()!=current.field(src).ok() { return Err(refuse("resuming extract each immutable scalar copies disagree with the current parent")); }
   180	        }
   181	        transfer(tx,image.field(destination)?,(&e.child,id,destination),(&e.child,id,destination))?;
   182	        count+=1;Ok(())
   183	    })?;
   184	    report.backfills.push(format!("extract {} each: resumed {count} current child rows without replaying consumed source lists",e.child));
   185	    Ok(())
   186	}
   187	
   188	pub(super) fn forward(
   189	    tx: &Tx<'_>,
   190	    e: &Extraction,
   191	    parent: &Table,
   192	    child: &Table,
   193	    key: &str,
   194	    report: &mut super::super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport,
   195	) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   196	    setup(tx)?;
   197	    let pt = tx.num(&e.parent)?;
   198	    let ct = tx.num(&e.child)?;
   199	    let (destination, source) = mapping(e, child);
   200	    validate_all_receipts(tx,key,pt,ct)?;
   201	    let mut count = 0u64;
   202	    super::super::activation_walk::walk_metered(tx, pt, crate::rows::adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, mut row, meter| {
   203	        let Some(list) = read_value(tx, pt, id, &row, source, meter)? else {
   204	            return Err(refuse(format!(
   205	                "extract each source {}.{source} is absent",
   206	                e.parent
   207	            )));
   208	        };
   209	        let values = match &list {
   210	            Value::Array(values) => values.as_slice(),
   211	            Value::Null
   212	                if parent
   213	                    .columns
   214	                    .get(source)
   215	                    .is_some_and(ColumnType::is_optional) =>
   216	            {
   217	                &[]
   218	            }
   219	            _ => return Err(refuse("extract each source must be a bounded asset list")),
   220	        };
   221	        let mut seen = BTreeSet::new();
   222	        for (position, v) in values.iter().enumerate() {
   223	            meter.charge(MeterKind::RowsWritten, 1)?;
   224	            meter.heap(1024)?; // retained seen-set node/IDs, before cloning them
   225	            let child_scratch=meter.mark();
   226	            let asset_id = asset_id(v)?;
   227	            if !seen.insert(asset_id.clone()) {
   228	                return Err(refuse(
   229	                    "extract each cannot put a repeated asset in two child homes; remove the duplicate element in an authorized source edit",
   230	                ));
   231	            }
   232	            let saved:Option<String>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT child_id FROM _extraction_each_rows WHERE definition=?1 AND parent_t=?2 AND parent_id=?3 AND asset_id=?4",params![key,pt,id,asset_id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   233	            let child_id = saved.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| {
   234	                crate::value::digest(&("extract-each-child", key, pt, id, &asset_id))
   235	            });
   236	            let present = read_image(tx, ct, &child_id, meter, false)?;
   237	            if saved.is_some() {
   238	                if present.is_none() || !anchored(tx, ct, &child_id, key, pt, id)? {
   239	                    return Err(refuse(
   240	                        "returning extract each lacks its receipt-owned child or valid parent-lifetime evidence; never recreate pruned/deleted history",
   241	                    ));
   242	                }
   243	            } else {
   244	                let independent:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _extraction_each_rows WHERE child_t=?1 AND child_id=?2) OR EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _extraction_rows WHERE child_t=?1 AND child_id=?2)",params![ct,child_id],|r|r.get(0))?;
   245	                if present.is_some() || independent {
   246	                    return Err(refuse(format!(
   247	                        "extract each destination {}/{child_id} already has independent history",
   248	                        e.child
   249	                    )));
   250	                }
   251	            }
   252	            let mut image = present.unwrap_or_else(|| {
   253	                Value::Row(
   254	                    BTreeMap::from([
   255	                        ("id".into(), Value::Id(child_id.clone())),
   256	                        (e.reference.clone(), Value::Id(id.into())),
   257	                    ])
   258	                    .into(),
   259	                )
   260	            });

     1	//! Preflight activation pages before owning their serialized row images.
     2	use super::*;
     3	use crate::{error::MeterKind, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{adoption, word_heap as heap}};
     4	
     5	pub(super) fn walk(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
     6	    walk_metered(tx, table, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, row, _| visit(id, row))
     7	}
     8	
     9	// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.1 — reserve stored bytes before fetching or decoding; page scratch stays live until consumed.
    10	pub(super) fn walk_metered(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work, scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value) -> crate::value::InternalPrice>,
    11	    visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    12	    let checked=|row:&Value|Ok(scratch.map_or_else(crate::value::InternalPrice::zero,|f|f(row)));
    13	    walk_metered_checked(tx,table,work,scratch.map(|_|&checked as &dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>),visit)
    14	}
    15	
    16	/// Row-specific provenance can fail closed before any page callback runs.
    17	pub(super) fn walk_metered_checked(tx: &Tx<'_>, table: i64, work: adoption::Work,
    18	    scratch: Option<&dyn Fn(&Value)->Result<crate::value::InternalPrice,RuntimeError>>,
    19	    mut visit: impl FnMut(&str, Value, &mut Meter) -> Result<(), RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
    20	    const PAGE: usize = 256;
    21	    let mut after: Option<String> = None;
    22	    loop {
    23	        // One job meter per page, shared with contribution evaluation. Retain
    24	        // the continuation's allowance across the page; reset only after all
    25	        // its row images and callback scratch have been consumed and dropped.
    26	        let mut meter = Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort);
    27	        meter.at("adopt stored rows");
    28	        meter.heap(after.as_deref().map_or(0, heap::text))?;
    29	        meter.once(crate::meter::HeapSite::SqliteColumns, 2 * tx.page_size)?;
    30	        let page_mark = meter.mark();
    31	        let mut limit = PAGE;
    32	        let mut page = loop {
    33	            limit = work.limit(&meter, limit);
    34	            let sizes = if after.is_some() {
    35	                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
    36	            } else {
    37	                "SELECT octet_length(id), octet_length(data) FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
    38	            };
    39	            let (mut count, mut bytes) = (0, 0u64);
    40	            {
    41	                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(sizes)?;
    42	                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, limit as i64])?;
    43	                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? {
    44	                    let id_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(0)? as usize;
    45	                    let data_bytes = row.get::<_, i64>(1)? as usize;
    46	                    // Rust-owned text, two native payload copies, allocation
    47	                    // headers and exact Vec slots. Length preflight borrows no
    48	                    // overflow payload (the same SQLite convention as scans).
    49	                    let next = bytes.saturating_add(3 * (heap::allocation(id_bytes) + heap::allocation(data_bytes)) + 64 + heap::slots::<(String, String)>(1));
    50	                    // This first size estimate bounds the fetch; admission below
    51	                    // also checks the actual decoder and contribution allowance.
    52	                    if count > 0 && next > meter.ceilings.heap_bytes / 2 { break; }
    53	                    meter.heap(next - bytes)?;
    54	                    bytes = next;
    55	                    count += 1;
    56	                }
    57	            }
    58	            if count == 0 { return Ok(()); }
    59	            let query = if after.is_some() {
    60	                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND id > ?2 ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
    61	            } else {
    62	                "SELECT id, data FROM r WHERE t = ?1 AND ?2 IS NULL ORDER BY id LIMIT ?3"
    63	            };
    64	            let mut page = Vec::<(String, String)>::with_capacity(count);
    65	            {
    66	                let mut statement = tx.tx.prepare_cached(query)?;
    67	                let mut rows = statement.query(params![table, after, count as i64])?;
    68	                while let Some(row) = rows.next()? { page.push((row.get(0)?, row.get(1)?)); }
    69	            }
    70	            if adoption::page_fits_checked(page.iter().map(|(_, data)| data.as_str()), &mut meter, work, scratch)? { break page; }
    71	            limit = (page.len() / 2).max(1);
    72	            drop(page);
    73	            meter.release_heap(page_mark.clone());
    74	        };
    75	        for (id, data) in &page {
    76	            let row_mark = meter.mark();
    77	            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
    78	            // Decode plus copy-on-write projection, optional materialization
    79	            // and validation scratch. Contribution keys reserve more through
    80	            // this same meter, on top of the still-resident complete page.
    81	            adoption::charge(&mut meter, adoption::decode_bytes(data))?;
    82	            let row = serde_json::from_str(data).map_err(|e| RuntimeError::Store(e.to_string()))?;
    83	            visit(id, row, &mut meter)?;
    84	            meter.release_heap(row_mark);
    85	        }
    86	        // Move the last ID into the next page's continuation, without an
    87	        // unpriced clone. Drop every other fetched byte before releasing.
    88	        after = page.pop().map(|row| row.0);
    89	        drop(page);
    90	        meter.release_heap(page_mark);
    91	    }
    92	}

    95	mod tests {
    96	    use super::*;
    97	
    98	    fn fixture(count: usize, payload: usize) -> Store {
    99	        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
   100	        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
   101	        for n in 0..count {
   102	            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
   103	            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
   104	                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(payload))),
   105	            ]));
   106	            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
   107	        }
   108	        tx.commit().unwrap();
   109	        store
   110	    }
   111	
   112	    #[test]
   113	    fn callback_keeps_the_whole_fetched_page_reserved() {
   114	        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
   115	        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
   116	        let mut serialized_bytes = 0;
   117	        for n in 0..20 {
   118	            let id = format!("r{n:04}");
   119	            let data = canonical_json(&crate::value::object([
   120	                ("id", Value::Id(id.clone())), ("payload", Value::String("x".repeat(1024))),
   121	            ]));
   122	            serialized_bytes += (id.len() + data.len()) as u64;
   123	            tx.tx.execute("INSERT INTO r(t,id,data) VALUES(1,?1,?2)", params![id, data]).unwrap();
   124	        }
   125	        let mut calls = 0;
   126	        let mut first_heap = None;
   127	        walk_metered(&tx, 1, adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |_, row, meter| {
   128	            let mut decoder = Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort);
   129	            adoption::charge(&mut decoder, adoption::decode_bytes(&canonical_json(&row)))?;
   130	            let decode = decoder.used.heap_bytes;
   131	            // Independent lower bound: Rust-owned images and both native
   132	            // payload copies coexist with decode. This excludes headers/slots,
   133	            // so it does not just repeat the walker's allocation formula.
   134	            assert!(meter.used.heap_bytes >= 3 * serialized_bytes + decode,
   135	                "Rust and native page copies must coexist with decoder scratch: {:?}", meter.used);
   136	            let expected = first_heap.get_or_insert(meter.used.heap_bytes);
   137	            assert_eq!(meter.used.heap_bytes, *expected, "earlier rows release only their own scratch");
   138	            assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, 20 + calls + 1, "all admission decodes plus callback decodes are charged");
   139	            calls += 1;
   140	            Ok(())
   141	        }).unwrap();
   142	        assert_eq!(calls, 20);
   143	        tx.rollback().unwrap();
   144	    }
   145	
   146	    #[test]
   147	    fn heap_retries_keep_admission_examinations_at_the_callback_boundary() {
   148	        let mut store = fixture(5, 1280 << 10);
   149	        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
   150	        let work = adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 1, index_entries: 1 };
   151	        let scratch = |row: &Value| adoption::contribution_bytes(row, &crate::schema::public_table(Default::default(), Default::default()));
   152	        let mut observed = Vec::new();
   153	        walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, Some(&scratch), |_, row, meter| {
   154	            observed.push(meter.used.examined);
   155	            assert_eq!((meter.used.steps, meter.used.index_entries), (0, 0), "rejected attempts perform no contribution work");
   156	            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
   157	            meter.step(1)?;
   158	            adoption::charge(meter, scratch(&row))?;
   159	            meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, 1)?;
   160	            assert!(meter.heap_peak <= Ceilings::job().heap_bytes);
   161	            Ok(())
   162	        }).unwrap();
   163	        // Four two-image attempts each decode one row before the heap check
   164	        // asks for a singleton. Its admission and callback decode add two.
   165	        // The last source is already a singleton and needs no rejected attempt.
   166	        assert_eq!(observed, [3, 3, 3, 3, 2]);
   167	        tx.rollback().unwrap();
   168	    }
   169	
   170	    #[test]
   171	    fn every_cumulative_budget_fixes_the_page_before_any_callback() {
   172	        for (work, sizes) in [
   173	            (adoption::Work { examined: 64, steps: 63, index_entries: 63 }, vec![252, 4]),
   174	            (adoption::Work { examined: 2, steps: 5040, index_entries: 1 }, vec![198, 58]),
   175	            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 0, index_entries: 400 }, vec![40, 40, 40, 40, 40, 40, 16]),
   176	        ] {
   177	            let mut store = fixture(256, 0);
   178	            let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
   179	            let (mut page, mut offset, mut calls) = (0, 0u64, 0);
   180	            walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, None, |_, _, meter| {
   181	                assert_eq!(meter.used.examined, sizes[page] + offset * work.examined + 1,
   182	                    "the entire admission pass and every earlier callback remain charged");
   183	                assert_eq!(meter.used.steps, offset * work.steps);
   184	                assert_eq!(meter.used.index_entries, offset * work.index_entries);
   185	                meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, work.examined - 1)?;
   186	                meter.step(work.steps)?;
   187	                meter.charge(MeterKind::IndexEntries, work.index_entries)?;
   188	                calls += 1;
   189	                offset += 1;
   190	                if offset == sizes[page] { page += 1; offset = 0; }
   191	                Ok(())
   192	            }).unwrap();
   193	            assert_eq!(calls, 256);
   194	            assert_eq!(page, sizes.len());
   195	            tx.rollback().unwrap();
   196	        }
   197	    }
   198	
   199	    #[test]
   200	    fn only_non_fitting_singletons_refuse_before_callbacks() {
   201	        for (work, kind) in [
   202	            (adoption::Work { examined: 16_384, steps: 0, index_entries: 0 }, MeterKind::Examined),
   203	            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 1_000_001, index_entries: 0 }, MeterKind::Steps),
   204	            (adoption::Work { examined: 1, steps: 0, index_entries: 16_385 }, MeterKind::IndexEntries),
   205	        ] {
   206	            for count in [0, 1] {
   207	                let mut store = fixture(count, 0);
   208	                let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
   209	                let mut calls = 0;
   210	                let result = walk_metered(&tx, 1, work, None, |_, _, _| { calls += 1; Ok(()) });
   211	                assert_eq!(calls, 0, "no callback can start with an impossible page budget");
   212	                if count == 0 { result.unwrap(); }
   213	                else { assert!(matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: got, .. }) if got == kind), "{result:?}"); }
   214	                tx.rollback().unwrap();
   215	            }
   216	        }
   217	    }

   350	
   351	pub(super) fn backward(
   352	    tx: &Tx<'_>,
   353	    e: &Extraction,
   354	    parent: &Table,
   355	    current_parent: &Table,
   356	    child: &Table,
   357	    key: &str,
   358	    report: &mut super::super::adoption_report::AdoptionReport,
   359	) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
   360	    setup(tx)?;
   361	    let pt = tx.num(&e.parent)?;
   362	    let ct = tx.num(&e.child)?;
   363	    let (destination, source) = mapping(e, child);
   364	    validate_all_receipts(tx,key,pt,ct)?;
   365	    let Some(kind) = parent.columns.get(source) else {
   366	        return Ok(());
   367	    };
   368	    if item_type(kind) != Some(&child.columns[destination]) {
   369	        return Err(refuse(
   370	            "reverse extract each requires identical asset element types in the old bounded list",
   371	        ));
   372	    }
   373	    // Retained populated children are not in the active snapshot. Their homes
   374	    // must never disappear from a reconstructed list. Restore their domain in
   375	    // an explicit loosened child bridge first; migration-owned nulls have no
   376	    // live child asset home and remain safely dormant.
   377	    let hidden:i64=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT COUNT(*) FROM _assets a WHERE a.home_table=?1 AND a.home_column=?2 AND a.released_at IS NULL AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _dormant_narrow_rows n WHERE n.t=?3 AND n.id=a.home_id) AND NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM r WHERE r.t=?3 AND r.id=a.home_id)",params![e.child,destination,ct],|r|r.get(0))?;
   378	    if hidden>0 { return Err(refuse(format!("reverse extract each cannot omit {hidden} retained narrowed child asset homes in {}; deploy a loosened child bridge that restores these rows before selecting old source",e.child))); }
   379	    // Index the assembled snapshot once on disk; never rescan all children
   380	    // for each parent or keep the whole store in process memory.
   381	    tx.conn().execute_batch("CREATE TEMP TABLE _extraction_each_children(parent_id TEXT NOT NULL,child_id TEXT NOT NULL,PRIMARY KEY(parent_id,child_id)) WITHOUT ROWID;")?;
   382	    super::super::activation_walk::walk_metered(tx, ct, crate::rows::adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |child_id, image, meter| {
   383	        if let Ok(Value::Id(parent_id)) = image.field(&e.reference) {
   384	            meter.heap(
   385	                (child_id.len() as u64 + parent_id.len() as u64)
   386	                    .saturating_mul(8)
   387	                    .saturating_add(256),
   388	            )?;
   389	            tx.conn().execute(
   390	                "INSERT INTO temp._extraction_each_children VALUES(?1,?2)",
   391	                params![parent_id, child_id],
   392	            )?;
   393	        }
   394	        Ok(())
   395	    })?;
   396	    let mut count = 0u64;
   397	    super::super::activation_walk::walk_metered(tx, pt, crate::rows::adoption::Work::DECODE, None, |id, mut row, meter| {
   398	        let mut children = Vec::new();
   399	        let mut membership=tx.conn().prepare("SELECT octet_length(child_id),child_id FROM temp._extraction_each_children WHERE parent_id=?1 ORDER BY child_id")?;
   400	        let mut members = membership.query([id])?;
   401	        while let Some(member) = members.next()? {
   402	            meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, 1)?;
   403	            let id_bytes:i64=member.get(0)?;
   404	            meter.heap((id_bytes.max(0) as u64).saturating_mul(8).saturating_add(512))?;
   405	            let child_id: String = member.get(1)?;
   406	            let image = read_image(tx, ct, &child_id, meter, false)?.ok_or_else(|| {
   407	                refuse("extract each child disappeared from its activation snapshot")
   408	            })?;
   409	            let child_id = child_id.as_str();
   410	            if !anchored(tx, ct, child_id, key, pt, id)? {
   411	                return Err(refuse(
   412	                    "extract each child lacks valid parent-lifetime evidence; refusing deletion/id reuse or retrospective recapture",
   413	                ));
   414	            }
   415	            if let Some(through) = &child.through {
   416	                let parent_image = crate::schema::row_image(&row, current_parent);
   417	                if through.fields.iter().any(|(capture, field)| {
   418	                    image.field(capture).ok() != parent_image.field(field).ok()
   419	                }) {
   420	                    return Err(refuse(
   421	                        "reverse extract each would change captured parent authority; preserve the child audience in an authored bridge",
   422	                    ));
   423	                }
   424	            }
   425	            let asset_value=image.field(destination)?;
   426	            meter.heap(asset_value.retained_heap_bytes().saturating_mul(4).saturating_add(512))?;
   427	            let v = asset_value.clone();
   428	            let asset_id = asset_id(&v)?;
   429	            let position:Option<i64>=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT position FROM _extraction_each_rows WHERE definition=?1 AND child_t=?2 AND child_id=?3",params![key,ct,child_id],|r|r.get(0)).optional()?;
   430	            children.push((
   431	                position.unwrap_or(i64::MAX),
   432	                child_id.to_owned(),
   433	                image,
   434	                v,
   435	                asset_id,
   436	            ));
   437	        }
   438	        drop(members);
   439	        drop(membership);
   440	        children.sort_by(|a, b| (&a.0, &a.1).cmp(&(&b.0, &b.1)));
   441	        let mut values = Vec::new();
   442	        let mut seen = BTreeSet::new();
   443	        for (position, (_, child_id, mut image, v, asset_id)) in children.into_iter().enumerate() {
   444	            meter.charge(MeterKind::RowsWritten, 1)?;
   445	            if !seen.insert(asset_id.clone()) {
   446	                return Err(refuse(
   447	                    "extract each found competing child homes for one asset",
   448	                ));
   449	            }
   450	            for (dest, src) in &e.fields {
   451	                if dest != destination && row.field(src).ok() != image.field(dest).ok() {
   452	                    return Err(refuse(
   453	                        "extract each immutable scalar copies disagree with the current parent",
   454	                    ));
   455	                }
   456	            }
   457	            transfer(
   458	                tx,
   459	                &v,
   460	                (&e.child, &child_id, destination),
   461	                (&e.parent, id, source),
   462	            )?;
   463	            fields(&mut image)?.remove(destination);
   464	            write(tx, ct, &child_id, &image)?;
   465	            tx.conn().execute(
   466	                "DELETE FROM _dormant_values WHERE t=?1 AND id=?2 AND column_name=?3",
   467	                params![ct, child_id, destination],
   468	            )?;
   469	            save(tx, key, pt, id, ct, &child_id, &asset_id, position)?;
   470	            values.push(v);
   471	            count += 1;
   472	        }
   473	        let was_null:bool=tx.conn().query_row("SELECT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM _extraction_cells WHERE definition=?1 AND parent_t=?2 AND parent_id=?3 AND column_name=?4 AND data=?5)",params![key,pt,id,source,canonical_json(&Value::Null)],|r|r.get(0))?;
   474	        let list = if values.is_empty()
   475	            && kind.is_optional()
   476	            && was_null
   477	        {
   478	            Value::Null
   479	        } else {
   480	            Value::Array(values)
   481	        };
   482	        remember(tx, key, pt, id, source, &list, meter)?;
   483	        fields(&mut row)?.insert(source.into(), list);
   484	        write(tx, pt, id, &row)?;
   485	        Ok(())
   486	    })?;
   487	    tx.conn()
   488	        .execute_batch("DROP TABLE temp._extraction_each_children;")?;
   489	    report.backfills.push(format!(
   490	        "extract {} each: moved {count} child asset homes back into parent lists",
   491	        e.child
   492	    ));
   493	    Ok(())
   494	}

     2	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000#20-visible-rollback-views-roll — deterministic identities, no upload or second home.

     7	    meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter},
